## Giant vulture

Giant vultures were a species of larger than normal vultures.

Description
Much like a standard-sized vulture, these creatures had pink bald heads and greasy blue-black feathers that covered their bodies.

Behavior
Giant vultures were often known to be malevolent as well as unusually intelligent for a bird. Far more fierce than their smaller cousins and less liable to be frightened away by injured prey. Upon sighting anything on the ground that appeared injured or dead, a giant vulture would quickly swoop down to investigate.

Biology
Giant vultures had a keen eyesight comparable to that of any eagle, falcon, or hawk. Due to their dietary habits, they possessed a natural resistance to many forms of disease as well as organic poisons.

These creatures typically laid around five to eight eggs, which would hatch within a span of two months. Their hatchlings were not capable of flight until at least four months of age.

History
The Sea Maidens Faire kept a giant vulture aboard the sailing ship the Heartbreaker, as part of their menagerie.

Ecology
Giant vultures lived in roaming flocks, typically of up to twelve members.
Diets
Like all species of vultures, these creatures were carrion eaters. They fed upon creatures that were either injured or already dead. Though they were also liable to attack creatures that were unconscious, restrained, or sleeping.
Habitats
Giant vultures typically inhabited high altitudes, such as mountains, within temperate or tropical regions. In Faerûn they were known to be found around parts of the Dalelands, Cormyr, and Sembia. In Maztica, they were known to be encountered all across its deserts.

In the Hordelands they could occasionally be found at the Katakoro Plateau, as well as in the land's following mountain ranges - A-Ling Shan, Chigiogi, Dustwall, Hagga Shan, Katakoro Shan, Khopet-Dag, Kora Shan, Kun-Yen Shan, Raurin Alta, Teyla Shan, Ulhai Shan, Yehimal.

In the Zakhara region's Crowded Sea, they could be found on the island chain of Nada al-Hazan and the archipelago of Jazayir al-Sartan.

Relationships
Some races were known to domesticate giant vultures. They were capable of being trained to stay within a specific region and to associate specific creatures with food, though only if they were raised since a young age. Their eggs and hatchlings typically fetched prices of around 30 to 60 gp.

Trivia
The spell flight of Remnis could summon a giant vulture to the aid of its caster.

## Giant sundew

Giant sundew was a species of dangerous almost-sentient carnivorous plant. It was a giant relative of ordinary sundews and a cousin of red sundews.

Description
A typical giant sundew reached up to  tall. From afar, they appeared as a mound of grayish-green ropes, cloth, and rags. They emitted a thick, sweet, syrupy smell that attracted flies, which would swarm the plant and be a telltale sign of lurking danger.

Combat
The sticky-sweet secretions that covered the plant's body protected it from missiles and fire attacks.

The giant sundew detected its prey's movements via vibrations. It laid low and inactive until something moved within  from its body. Then it instinctively attacked with up to six tendrils per creature at a time. The plant's tendrils secreted sticky-sweet sap that restrained its prey. The sap was also corrosive, slowly damaging the unfortunate victim.

Ecology
The sundews grew in damp and shaded areas. They were attached to the ground via hair-like thin roots. The plant was able to uproot itself and slowly move to a new hunting spot using its sticky tendrils.

The sap could be dangerous on its own even without the sundew's attacks. If the prey was attacked in the face with the sundew's sap they could die of asphyxiation within 12–24 seconds. The only non-magical way to dissolve the dangerous sap was soaking it with vinegar or alcohol.

## Gimblebock

Gimblebock was the leader of a group of looters in the late 15 century DR.

Description
The gnome had swept-back blonde hair and wore a suit of scale mail armor. He fought with a greatsword that was similar in height to him.

History
In the, Gimblebock led a band of looters including Andorn, Barton, Cefrey, Haseid, Mari, Quelenna, Taman, Torgga, and Warryn. They tried to break into an ancient crypt near the shore of the River Channath. During their excavation, a nautiloid crashed close by, and the gnome immediately laid claim to it. Taman argued with him, saying they didn't know for sure what the nature of the crashed object was, but Gimblebock was sure that it was a ship.

## Giant tick

Giant ticks were massive bloodsucking insects.

Description
Some described these parasites as being the size of boars.

Ability
Much like their smaller cousins, giant ticks were known to carry fatal diseases that could kill a victim within 2–4 days.

Combat
Giant ticks hid on elevated rock formations and trees waiting for a warm-blooded prey to drop onto. On a successful bite with their hollow mouth-tube, the giant tick attached to the victim draining its blood until the insect was full and bloated.

After a giant tick attached to its victim, it had to be killed, burned, or immersed in water for it to detach before it reached satiation.

History
In 1357 DR and up to 1368 DR, giant ticks could occasionally be found in the sewers beneath Waterdeep.

Ecology
Diet
These creatures were willing to consume the blood of any creature they could catch, including giants.
Habitats
Giant ticks were commonly found in forests and occasionally in caves or caverns. In the Shining South, they were known to inhabit the Mortick Swamp. They could also be found in many of Cormanthor's forests, particularly in the Vale of Lost Voices. In Interior Faerûn, they could be found in the jungle within the Cathedral of Emerald Scales.

## Giant hornet

Giant hornets were aggressive massive mutations of normal-sized hornets.

Description
Giant hornets were flying carnivorous insects with long bodies, a fell stinger, and an average wingspan of  or more.

Behavior
Giant hornets were solitary but extremely aggressive insects, rarely encountered in groups. They always attacked on sight.

Fire had a weakening effect on these giant insects, and smoke was a repellent.

Combat
Giant hornets swooped down on their prey, pinning and holding a victim with their legs while repeatedly stabbing them with their poisonous stinger. The poison in the stinger caused 2–6 hours of paralyzation.

Fortunately, the hornets' buzzing was loud enough to be heard  away in dungeon corridors and from half as far outside, preventing unexpected and stealthy attacks.

Ecology
They were carnivorous, just like their normal-sized relatives. Unlike bees, hornets did not lose their stingers and lives after an attack.
Habitats
The giant hornets' natural habitats were coniferous forests and, on occasion, underground caves.

In north Faerûn giant hornets could be found in Thar, the rimwood area of Cormanthor, the King's Forest of Cormyr, Mistledale, Shadowdale, and around the Pool of Yeven.

Beyond Faerun, giant hornets were known to inhabit various mountains and hills in Maztica. In east Zakhara, they could be found in the Grey Jungle. And in the Crowded Sea south of Zakhara, giant hornets could be found on the Djinni's Claws and Nada al-Hazan island chains.

## Gith and Slaad

Gith and Slaad was described by some scholars as the first layer of Limbo. Named after its most numerous inhabitants, the githzerai and the slaadi, the layer was scarcely distinguishable from the rest of the plane.

Description
Like all of Limbo, Gith and Slaad was a churning storm of ever-changing matter and energy, dotted with fragments of temporarily stable landscape. The raw chaos could be manipulated and stabilized by a creature's force of will.

Cosmography
Most of the portals in and out of Limbo were located in this layer, usually leading to Ysgard, Pandemonium, and the Outlands. They could assume any form, but were usually surrounded by slowly rotating areas of stable matter. However, these portals were highly unreliable, often leading to random locations.

The barriers separating Gith and Slaad from the other layers of Limbo were considerably weaker than in other planes, so that it was possible to find divine realms typically associated with other layers of the plane there, as well as permanent settlements throughout the plane. Those barriers were also constantly shifting their position, sometimes allowing portions of a neighboring layer to bleed through, increasing or decreasing the layer's size. Many scholars simply disregarded the reason for the barriers' existence, considering the entire plane one single layer.

Notable Locations
 The githzerai settlements of the Floating City and Shra'kt'lor, home to Zaerith Menyar-Ag-Gith, the Great Githzerai, were occasionally located in this layer as they moved through the plane.
 The realm of the slaad lord Ygorl was occasionally found in this layer as it randomly traveled throughout the plane.

## Giant praying mantis

Giant praying mantises, also called gargantuan praying mantises, were larger versions of praying mantises.

Description
Giant praying mantises, like their smaller cousins, were camouflaged to look like the leaves of plants.

Combat
These carnivorous insects could stand motionless, hidden in foliage, until they struck out silently on approaching prey. They gripped their prey tightly in their claws and then bit down with their mandibles.

Ecology
Habitats
In Faerûn they could be found in the forest of Cormanthor. Beyond Faerûn, they could be found in parts of Kara-Tur.

Beyond Toril, they were among the most dangerous predators of equatorial Karpri.
Relationships
The gargantuan praying mantises of Cormanthor were the primary predators of the forest's giant stag beetles.

## Giant bloodworm

Giant bloodworms were large sanguivores that resembled a giant earthworm.

Description
Giant bloodworms had spotted dark green, slimy skin and a dark brown underbelly. They typically measured  in length. In the dimlight of their underground habitats, they could often be mistaken by other creatures for a moss-covered boulder.

Biology
Giant bloodworms propelled their bodies along the ground of their watery habitats, being incapable of swimming.

They were particularly vulnerable to fire.

Combat
If hungry or provoked, a giant bloodworm attacked by attempting to affix its mouth to a target. Once attached the worm would continue to suck blood from its victim until it was satiated, the victim died, or it was dislodged.

History
In 1357 DR, giant bloodworms could occasionally be found in the sewers beneath Waterdeep.

Ecology
Diet
Giant bloodworms were a sanguivorous species.
Habitats
Giant bloodworms were typically known to inhabit shallow pools of water within underground or caverns.

## Giant-killer

Giant-killers, otherwise known as giant slayers, were adventurers who devoted their lives to hunting and killing giants and giant-kin, usually driven by a need to avenge some past deed committed by one. Elves and dwarves were the most common races to take up this way of life.

Abilities
Giant-killers were known to be very agile fighters, skilled in targeting the weak points of their foes. Due to their narrow obsession with giants, they tended to be very knowledgeable about how to track down or sneak up on them, but also how to tailor their attacks to whichever race of giant they were facing. These tactics were also applicable to ogres and trolls.

Many of the tactics that giant-killers in Faerun utilized were developed by shield dwarfs. On the isle of Norland, giant-killers utilized techniques that were developed by Northlanders.

Possessions
Giant-killers were known to often wear over-sized armor. Many sought out magical girdles and gauntlets of strength, inspired by old legends. They typically utilized two-handed weapons, such as bastard swords and battleaxes, as well as hurled weapons. Bows, crossbows, and slings were viewed by some as being weapons fit for only cowards.The book does not specify that the "magical girdles" are girdles of giant strength, though due to the nature of this kit being focused around giants it can be assumed. Some were known to decorate their shields with ribbons and medals awarded to them by grateful communities.

Activities
Giant-killers generally traveled alone or in small groups.

Some good-aligned giant killers were known to linger on killing good-aligned giants, waiting until they did something that would warrant their death. However, understandably all giants regardless of their alignment detested giant killers.

History
Many giant-killers arose and flocked across Faerun to the Evermoors in the,  when giants began to invade the region and displaced its troll population into the Silver Marches. Around this time they were considered an invaluable asset in the region's outskirts, especially in the city of Nesmé. However, some were more driven by greed than an obsession to slay giants and would refuse to lend their services for low rewards.

Around this same time, the dwarven settlements of Citadel Felbarr and Mithral Hall were known to have warriors skilled in the art of giant killing, though those of the former rarely lent their services out to any other settlement.

After the island of Oman was overrun by fomorians in the, the Northlanders of the Moonshae Isles developed and began teaching techniques for giant-killing in hopes of one day reclaiming the island.

Notable Giant-killers

 Cazi Alphelandra
 Crag Ungart
 Ghelryn Foehammer
 Holper Stoutshield
 Helena Nostrum
 Lai Antolio
 Solara Thann
 Urgala Meltimer

Groups
 The Blood Riders
 Hook, Line, and Sinker
 Oman giantkillers
 The Savage Seven

Reputation
Giant-killers were equally as likely to receive adoration as they were to be targeted by local bullies and tough guys for harassment or to be considered a blowhard. This was due to them presenting themselves so aggressively, often having big egos, and frequently boasting of their latest triumphs.

## Giant sea horse

Giant sea horses were a large version of their smaller kin.

Description
They looked like large sea horses of flamboyant color, with a long body and a curled tail.

Behavior
Giant sea horses were naturally shy creatures and try to avoid contact with surface creatures.

Combat
Giant sea horses defended themselves by headbutting an opponent. They could be trained to use their strong tail as a weapon in melee combat.

Ecology
Diet
These creatures were a herbivorous species.
Habitats
Giant sea could potentially be found in nearly all but the coldest of oceans.

They could often be found in the seas and ocean around the Moonshae Isles.
Relationships
Tritons and giant sea horses had good relationships. They also were sea elves' favored mounts and could be trained in combat. Locathah were also known to occasionally capture and train them.

Giant sea horses were sometimes found as courtiers or servants in the palace of Kalbari al-Durrat al-Amwaj ibn Jari.

A giant sea horse could carry up to 450 pounds and drag up to 2,250 pounds.

Summoning
A giant seahorse could be summoned with the Summon monster II spell.

## Giant's Chalice

Giant's Chalice, otherwise known as Suswynfa by its octopoid inhabitants, was the largest known freshwater lake in the Lowerdark, within the Darklands domain.

Description
Giant's Chalice had a large U-shape, making it what some referred to as an oxbow lake.

Geography
In relation to the surface, Giant's Chalice was said to be located 12 miles beneath the Trader's Road and it was connected to the Sea of Fallen Stars. It was said to stretch from the southernmost tip of the Lake of Dragons, down to Asbravn, and then up to the Bridge of Fallen Men

In relation to the Lowerdark, it was located roughly north of Cairnheim.

History
Stone giants once dwelled above the Giant's Chalice and some believed that dwarves from Shanatar dubbed the lake "Giant's Chalice" because of this.

In 1357 DR, a group of mindflayers from Oryndoll introduced vampire squids to the lake's ecosystem. The blue ring octopuses who inhabited the lake would spend years fruitlessly trying to negotiate peace with their savage cousins, who often would feast upon them. Over time their population would greatly decline because of this.

By 1372 DR members of this race concluded that more active measures needed to be taken. They began honing their skills in combat and magic, while also appealing to their sea elf trading partners for aid in the conflict.

Flora & Fauna
Creatures in the Lowerdark were known to travel from miles away just to drink from this lake. Predatory creatures typically prowled around the lake for such prey.

Within its depths laid a glowing coral reef, whose light could be seen from above the water in some parts. These were tended to by blue ring octopuses, who some in 1372 DR estimated had been living there for over a millennium. They were known to carve this coral into intricate shapes and then trade them with sea elves traveling from the Sea of Fallen Stars through flooded passages. These sea elves often came from the kingdom of Naramyr and would resell them at the city of Myth Nantar.

## Girog

Girog was a hill giant who lived in Chief Groote's steading, located along the northern foothills of the Ice Spires, and was one of the highest ranking food gatherers in his community.

History
In 1366 DR, at a point when he was considered elderly for his race, Girog began routinely meeting in secret with some rogues from Hartsvale. He would purchase stag meat from them that they had poached from the royal forests of King Camden.

Girog traded this illegal meat with the glutton Gorgg, under the promise that he would be given the unusually tender foodstuffs he needed to win an ordning challenge and thus rise in the hill giant social strata to the class of glutton.

A girdle of fortitude was a magical belt that improved the wearer's hardiness and vitality for a limited duration.

## Giggle

Giggle was a person-affecting cantrip that beginning wizards could learn. It caused a target creature to suffer a brief fit of laughter.

Effects
This cantrip had a range of  and could target a single person or creature. If the target did not resist the spell, then it broke out in laughter for a period of two to three seconds before they could recover. If the spell was resisted, only a momentary chuckle ensued.

Components
This cantrip required verbal and somatic components to cast. The caster wiggled a finger rapidly back and forth while uttering a word or phrase.The verbal component of this cantrip was known as a "punch line".

## Giant snapping turtle

Giant snapping turtles were, as their name suggested, a huge-sized variety of snapping turtle and a species of giant turtle.

Description
Giant snapping turtles resembled regular snapping turtles in all respects, only much bigger, growing to diameters of  up to . Like normal turtles, they had a bony outer shell, divided into an upper half called a carapace and a lower half called a plastron. They were capable of withdrawing their more lightly armored head and legs partly or wholly into this shell for defense, but their heads could extend up to  long in the very largest specimens. Although they typically moved and swam slowly, they were capable of shocking speed when they attacked.

Abilities
Giant snapping turtles were amphibious.

Behavior
Known to be aggressive and ravenous in appetite, they were rightly feared for the danger they posed. They would snap at smaller creatures that came close.

Combat
Giant snapping turtles hunted by lying still at the bottom of a lake or river or close to shore, before they swiftly extended their necks to snatch their surprised prey, inflicting grievous injury—one could well bite a human in half. If the victim was grabbed and still lived, the giant snapping turtle kept biting, and it took a great feat of strength to free oneself. When the prey was subdued or slain, the giant snapping turtle threw its head back, tossed the prey a few feet in the air, then caught and swallowed it.

When attacked, the giant snapping turtle was too slow to flee or put up a fight, so it preferred to withdraw into its shell, which granted great protection. While some breeds were unable fully withdraw, they tucked their limbs against the shell to minimize exposure. They would then deliver deadly bites against the attackers. Their builds made them very stable, but when they were knocked on their backs, it took an effort to right themselves.

History
While traveling to the Vishap's Teeth in 1367 DR, Khafaz ibn Dahz and his caravan encountered two giant desert snapping turtles.

Sometime before 1372 DR, the expedition to the Lizard Marsh led by Emythene Faldree encountered one of these giant turtles of . Four of the group were killed by its snapping jaws. The detailed recount of the expedition could be found in one of the books published by Emythene. It was called Beware the Lizard Marsh! and could e found in Daggerford's city library.

Sub-Species
Giant Desert Snapping Turtle A species that was uniquely adapted to living on land, the undersides of their shells contained large pockets which they used to store water. They were endemic to the High Desert of Zakhara and their corpses could be found scattered all throughout it.

Ecology
While it varied with species, giant snapping turtles lived a long time, anywhere for thirty years to a hundred and fifty years.

They were typically solitary creatures.
Diet
Giant snapping turtles were omnivorous creatures, taking whatever they found in their environment, from all kinds of insects and fishes to plants and small mammals, and even humans. They had a preference for fresh green plants and live worms, and understandably avoided carrion or rotting plant matter except when starving.
Habitats
Giant snapping turtles dwelled in large freshwater lakes and rivers. Being uncommon but not rare, sightings of them were possibly the origins of some stories of lake monsters. It was possible that a desert-adapted giant snapping turtle might even exist.

Giant snapping turtles were one of the many dangers of the Lizard Marsh to the west of Daggerford.

Giant snapping turtles were found in the rivers of the Utter East, such as in Serpent Valley and Realm of Tides. They stayed in the water, and attacked all who came close to the shore. In the Unapproachable East, they could be found in the River Lapendrar.

They were also found in Snapping Turtle Bay in Chult, a place named for them. Throughout Chult, giant snapping turtles could be found in rivers and swamps, and sunning themselves on beaches. In the region of Kara-tur, they could be found on the Akari island.

Giant snapping turtles could also be found in remote parts of the Moonshae Isles and in Lake Sember.

Beyond Toril, these creatures could be found on the planet of Oerth.

Usages
Many cultures considered giant turtle meat a delicacy; it was tasty and nutritious.

The carapaces of giant turtles could be used to make sturdy roofs, small huts, and boats.

Notable Giant Snapping Turtles
 King Toba, a unique crystal-shelled giant snapping turtle in Snapping Turtle Bay in the late 15 century DR.
 Ol' Cholms, a giant snapping turtle reputed to live in Gray Harbor in Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR.

## Giant bee

Giant bees, or monstrous bees, were extra-large varieties of their smaller cousins.

Description
Giant bees could reach lengths of about .

Like their tiny cousins, giant bees came in multiple varieties, including honeybees and bumblebees. Of the two, giant bumblebees were the larger species.

Behavior

Giant bees were not ordinarily aggressive, unless they were acting in defense.

Abilities
Like normal-sized bees, a giant bee could inject a poison into another creature, but pulling away from the sting would result in the death of the giant bee. Giant bee venom weakened the general constitution of one stung, without harming the targets strength or agility. The venom of a giant bumblebee was stronger for giant soldier honeybees and giant bumblebees, and the poison from a queen was stronger still.

Giant bees were very skilled at orienting themselves in their environment.

Sub-Species
Upper Planes Giant Bee Giant bees that lived in the Upper Planes were celestial creatures, and more intelligent than normal giant bees. They served the good-aligned deities, being particularly favored by the halfling goddess Sheela Peryroyl, and were capable of being summoned for short periods of time by spellcasters with the spell Summon monster II.

Ecology
Giant bees maintained small hives, numbering up to 20 individuals in typical situations, but some giant honeybees had much larger hives numbering up to ten times that amount in workers with up to a dozen soldiers, two to five drones, and a single queen. Giant bumblebees kept burrowed nests instead of hives, with up to a dozen individuals.
Habitats
They could be found in parts of Kara-Tur.
Usages
Like smaller bees, giant bees produced honey. A chunk of honey known as "bee bread" was capable of providing daily nourishment for a typical humanoid. In rare cases, a variety known as "royal jelly" was found, which was said to have mild healing and curative properties and even the ability to slow aging! Jars of this coveted unguent could cost between 3,000 and 8,000 gold pieces.

Trivia
 Magical amber amulets of vermin were sometimes crafted on Toril that contained a small bee that would grow into a giant bee when the amulet was broken.

 Giant hornet
 Giant wasp

## Giant lynx

Giant lynxes were a rare species of large feline.

Description
Their muscular bodies were covered in thick fur. They had tufts of fur growing from the tops of their ears and cheeks. Their legs were strong, and they had very large paws, which allowed them to navigate in snowy conditions with ease.

Abilities
A giant lynx's powerful legs allowed it to jump a distance of up to . They had keen detection skills and nearly always detected the presence of a trap. They were stealthy creatures, almost always avoiding detection from prey or any humanoid who was hunting them.

Behavior
Giant lynxes were the most intelligent of the felines and were playful, free-spirited creatures.

Combat
A giant lynx's primary weapon was its sharp claws.

Habitat
Giant lynxes often lived in cold climates, usually in thick forests or scrubs. They were known to live in Icewind Dale and Rashemen. They had also been spotted in the Beastlands.

Giant lynxes were also common in the warmer climates of the Land of the Lions around the Lake of Steam and the Forest of Mir.

Ecology
Giant lynxes were nocturnal and hunted at night. Their diet mainly consisted of small prey, such as juvenile deer, grouse, and rodents. After birth, a young giant lynx cub remained with its mother for up to six months.

Giant lynxes had their own language. Some druids were able to speak this language and communicate with giant lynxes.

Relationships

Wood elves who lived in colder areas had a positive relationship with giant lynxes, who were considered companions and helped protect lairs and villages. Clerics of Rillifane Rallathil were able to summon giant lynxes to their side.

The Ala-min tribe of the Shadi'ar of Arnaden maintained an elite cavalry of between a half and two dozen riders on giant lynxes. These lynxes were obtained by an agreement with the elves of the Forest of Mir.

Uses
Giant lynxes were hunted for their pelts, which were often kept as trophies.

 Lynx

## Giragonne

Giragonnes were magical hybrids of giraffes and copper dragons. These creatures were natural comedians, and were always looking for an audience.

Description
Giragonnes combined traits of their component creatures, featuring the long neck and spindly legs of a giraffe, and the draconic head and wings of a copper dragon. Although it stood  tall, its torso was not much larger than that of a draft horse.

Combat
Giragonnes rarely engaged in combat, preferring to defuse a situation or incapacitate assailants using their natural ability to cast Otto's irresistible dance, grease, Tasha's hideous laughter, and baleful transposition, as well as their adhesive, thick spittle. If forced into a fight, giragonnes were able to deliver a powerful kick with their hind legs.

Society
Although giragonnes were sociable with other species, they generally didn't seek out the companionship of their kin.

Giragonne lairs were filled with objects the creatures deemed comedic, including books and scrolls filled with jokes.

Giragonnes laired mostly in the Shaar and Aunorach, though they lived throughout desert and savannah areas.

Rumors & Legends
Legend had it that these creatures were created by a humorous wizard with a passion for crossbreeding magical creatures.

Appendix

 Dragonne

## Giraffe

Giraffes were a species of exceptionally tall mammals.

Behavior
Like many herd animals, giraffes were a peaceful species and would flee in a stampede from whatever they perceived to be the greatest threat to their safety.

Combat
Giraffes were capable of delivering strong kicks with their hefty hooves.

Ecology
Habitats
Large herds of giraffes were known to roam the Chultan savannah. Exotic bongo drums were used on the Malatran Plateau in Kara-Tur. They were constructed using giraffe skin.

Trivia
Certain wands of wonder could summon giraffes.

## Giant cockroach

Giant cockroaches were giant insects found in the Underdark

Description
Giant cockroaches looked identical to their smaller cousins, just much larger. Their carapace was usually black, brown or grey in color and was covered in oily secretions.

Behavior
Giant cockroaches were extremely combative and hostile.

Abilities
Giant cockroaches were capable of detecting the presence of any creature in direct contact with the earth within . They had a limited flight ability over a distance of up to . The oily secretions that covered their carapace were so potent that any creature within  was overcome with nausea. Giant cockroaches were immune to all mind-affecting effects and spells.

Combat
Giant cockroaches attacked nearly any creature it encountered.

Ecology
Habitats
Giant cockroaches lived and reproduced in warm areas near water, surrounded by rotting organic material.

Beyond the Underdark, giant cockroaches could be found in the land of Zakhara.

Appearances

## Giant maggot

Giant maggots were the larval stage for several giant insect species.

Description
Giant maggots looked like their smaller cousins. Some giant maggots had several appendages growing from their body, causing them to resemble a hideous caterpillar.

Abilities
Giant maggots were immune to all mind-affecting effects and spells.

Combat
Giant maggots avoided combat, only attacking a creature in self-defense. If a giant maggot was starving, they became predatory and attacked nearby creatures.

Ecology
Several giant insect species laid their eggs on or near the decaying corpse of a large creature or a large pile of dung, upon which the giant maggots fed. When they hatched, giant maggots were  in length. They continued to grow for several weeks until reaching a maximum length of about . Shortly after, the giant maggot metamorphosed into an adult giant insect.

## Gist

The Gist family was one of the noble patriar families of Baldur's Gate. They owed their wealth to their ownership of the majority of the city's production of dyes.

Members
Felonius Gist, the 14 century art dealer, who once purchased six stone statues that were in fact petrified adventurers.
Haverford Gist, a charitable nobleman in the late 15 century DR.
Leander Gist, a patriar from the late 15 century DR.

## Giant water spider

Giant water spiders, also known as monstrous diving spiders, were a common species of monstrous spider that were adapted to living in aquatic environments.

Description
These spiders came in a wide variety of sizes, similar to those of ordinary monstrous spiders, but generally tended to be smaller. One of the more common sizes for them was around .

Behavior
Giant water spiders possessed a slight degree of intelligence. Because of this, creatures such as nixies were sometimes known to establish relations with them, often by means of offering food. But like any spider, they were liable to attack any creature that disturbed their home.

Abilities
These creatures possessed a powerful poisonous bite. Their bodies were well adapted for running across all vegetative and mineral surfaces underwater, as well as swimming. They also possessed a sort of tremorsense, allowing them to detect and pinpoint other creatures within water.

Unlike other spiders, these creatures could not nets of webbing to use against their foes and their spidersilk lacked the sticky quality found in other species.

Sub-Species
Giant marine spiders A rare species of water spider that was larger than others and found only in saltwater environments.

Ecology
Lairs
Giant water spiders would build large nests, either in a burrow amidst underwater vegetation or with spidersilk, that acted as a pocket of air. Any prey that was caught while out hunting would be dragged back to this lair, where they could safely cocoon them for later digestion.

Habitats
These creatures were only known to inhabit freshwater lakes. In particular, giant water spiders were known to occasionally be found in the lakes of Cormanthor, the neighboring Moonsea region's lake of the same name, the River Ashaba, Dragon Reach, and the Marsh of Chelimber. Beneath the cold plains and Galena Mountains of the Vaasa region, they inhabited an Underdark cavern sacred to Kiaransalee known as the Vault of Gnashing Teeth.

Following the Spellplague, giant water spiders could be found in the Vilhon Reach.

In the region of Kara-tur these creatures could be found in its temperate swamps and bodies of water, such as the rivers on Akari island.

Summoning
A Fiendish Giant water spider ally could be summoned with the spells; summon monster I, summon monster II, summon monster IV, summon monster VI, summon monster VIII and summon monster IX for increasingly larger specimens in lieu of terrestrial specimens when on the water.

Appendix
Appearances

Connections

## Giant gar

Giant gars were a rare, larger-than-normal variety of gar.

Description
These creatures had long, sleek, and aerodynamic bodies that measured  in length. They had small fins but were covered in large, hard, overlapping scales. Their mouths were similar to those of an alligator or crocodile and contained needle-sharp teeth.

Behavior
Giant gars were comparatively far more aggressive than their smaller cousins. They were largely fearless and considered anything small enough to swallow to be prey.

Combat
These creatures were capable of swallowing whole their victims, as well as objects up to  in length. Even giant gars that were considered to be small in size were capable of swallowing whole elves and dwarves.

History
A giant gar was one of many creatures installed as guardians in the Temple of Gulkulath within Undermountain.

Sub-Species
Blackfins A species of giant gar that inhabited areas with cold water. They were known to be found around some areas of the Moonshae Isles.

Ecology
Giant gars lived in groups known as schools, typically comprising up to six members.

Habitats
These creatures were known to inhabit deep freshwater lakes and rivers in temperate regions.

In the land of Faerûn they could be found in the waters around Cormanthor, particularly in the River Ashaba. They also inhabited the River of Gems in Anauroch, the shallows of the Rat Hills, freshwater bodies in the Moonshae Isles, the Sea of Fallen Stars, that sea's northern fjord Dragon Reach, and Thay's River Lapendrar.

Beyond Faerûn, they could be found in the tropical and subtropical fresh waters of Kara-Tur. Within the Underdark, giant gars could be found in the upper lake of Deepearth.

Besides Toril, giant gars were known to be found on the planet of Oerth.

## Giant fly

Giant flies were monstrous varieties of the mundane order of insects known as flies.

Behavior
All species of giant fly were highly fearful of fire and instinctively avoided it at all cost, as it could easily destroy their wings.

Combat
Giant flies would land upon their prey and bite at their bodies. When engaging an aggressively defensive foe, giant flies tended to jump  backwards to escape combat.

Species
Bluebottle
A monstrous variety of the mundane bluebottle fly. They rarely attacked living creatures, unless they had open wounds or were covered in blood. This is because they preferred eating things such as carrion and offal. They were also known to be attracted to sweet odors. Those bitten by giant bluebottle flies had a low chance of contracting some form of disease.
Horsefly
A monstrous variety of the mundane horsefly, these were the largest species of giant fly. Highly aggressive, they were willing to attack nearly any creature. They had a tuberous mouth from which they drew blood from others.

Ecology
Habitats
Giant flies were primarily known to inhabit the jungles of Chult.

Giant horseflies were known to inhabit the rimwood forest of Cormanthyr, as well as the plains of Cormyr, the Dalelands, and Sembia. In Thay, they could be found in Delhumide and Priador. And beyond Toril, they could be found on the planet Karpri.

In Interior Faerûn, both giant horseflies and bluebottle flies could be found in the jungle within the Cathedral of Emerald Scales.

Trivia
 The figurine of wondrous power known as the "ebon fly" was carved in the likeness of a horsefly. When activated it transformed into a giant fly that could be ridden as a mount.
 Myshik Morueme was skilled in riding giant flies as mounts.

Appendix
See Also
 Assassin bug, a species of insect that closely resembled the giant bluebottle.
 Chasme, a type of tanar'ri demon whose lower body was that of a giant fly.

## Giant leech

Giant leeches were a monstrous variety of the mundane, segmented worms of the same name.

Description
These creatures had slimy, slug-like bodies that were either mottled brown or tan in hue, or occasionally gray. Their bodies measured  in length and had two antennae that protruded from their mouths. They had a round, sucker-like orifice for a mouth that allowed them to attach themselves to their prey.

On land these creatures moved very slowly in an inch-worm fashion, stretching forward and forward and attaching its anterior disk to the ground before doing the same with their posterior disk. They were even capable of climbing vertical and overhanging surfaces. When swimming however, giant leeches moved at a rapid pace and their bodies undulated vertically.

Behavior
Giant leeches never attacked for the sake of self-defense, only to feed. Whenever injured, they would attempt to flee the area.

Biology
Being an invertebrate species, giant leeches could squeeze their bodies through very small spaces. Even those who were rather larger were capable of this feat.
Senses
Sight Like many species of invertebrate, giant leeches had a set of ocelli in place of normal eyes. These were a group of light receptive cells that could register changes in light intensity, though not actual images. Because of this, giant leeches often reacted to changes in nearby light.

Scent Giant leeches had a keen sense of smell and could easily home in on or avoid certain creatures or substances, such as a bleeding animal. Their smell senses were sensitive to certain chemicals, even in small amounts.

In Water Giant leeches were very sensitive to vibrations, easily becoming restless, and would attempt to locate the source of the disturbance. They also were sensitive to traces of metal in their water, with copper being the most fatal.

When Feeding When attached to prey, giant leeches were unresponsive to most outside stimuli. The presence of ashes, fire, dehydrating substances, and especially salt would make them quickly release their grasp on a creature. They were highly vulnerable to salt and the application of sufficient amounts could even kill them. Because of this, giant leeches generally avoided surfaces covered in the mineral.
Saliva
Giant leeches had viscous saliva that was hard to remove without alcohol or a similar liquid. Their saliva had anesthetic properties, making it so that the creatures they attacked felt no pain and thus ensured they went unnoticed. This effect of their saliva was felt by their prey immediately upon contact.

The saliva of these creatures also contained an anti-coagulant and a substance known as a vasodilator that would enlarge blood vessels near wherever they bit. These induced a state of increased blood flow in their prey, ensuring that blood continued to flow from their bite for a duration longer than that of a normal wound. Their saliva also contained antibiotics, which made infections from their bites a rare occurrence.

Additionally, some varieties of giant leech had a substance within their saliva that would dissolve any pre-existing blood clots. When inside their digestive tract, blood did not coagulate.
Reproduction
Giant leeches were a hermaphroditic species, though they could not fertilize their own eggs. Some species laid them in cocoons, which they would attach to something underwater or bury within ooze. Others were known to carry their eggs around in a membranous sac on their underside.

Combat
These creatures took a stealthy and relaxed approach to hunting. Resting their bodies upon underwater plants and objects, or hiding within the mud or slime typical of their habitats, and waited to strike upon any prey that came within  of them. They sucked the blood from creatures they attacked and were only rarely noticed. Those bitten by giant leeches had a moderate chance of contracting some form of disease, in particular red ache.

History
In 1357 DR and up to 1368 DR, giant leeches could occasionally be found in the sewers beneath Waterdeep.

Around the 14 century DR, a healer in Skullport by the name of Leech employed tamed giant leeches to remove poisons from peoples' bodies.

Ecology
Diet
Much like their smaller counterparts, giant leeches primarily ate blood. Though some species were predacious, eating animals such as frogs and eels, and even the parasitic variety would engage in such eating habitats on occasion. Beyond that, there were some species of giant leech that fed upon carrion.

They generally hunted at night or whenever it was raining, in order to ensure that their bodies stayed moist. Once fed, they could go a whole month without feeding again and they could not be convinced to consume more once full. Because of this, they tended to shelter themselves after eating in a secure place where they could remain inactive for a period.
Habitats
These creatures were only known to be found in the waters of swamps and marshes.

In Faerûn, they inhabited the Mere of Dead Men, the Great Swamp of Rethild, the Stump Bog, the temperate swamps of Cormyr such as the Vast Swamp, temperate areas of the Sea of Fallen Stars, and the Lake of Steam. They were also quite prevalent in the marshes of High Moor, where they were found hunting in packs, and in the forests of Vaasa.

In the Unapproachable East, giant leeches could be found within the Umber Marshes of Aglarond, as well as around that nation's coastal regions.

Beyond Faerûn they could be found in temperate bodies of freshwater in Kara-Tur. Including the swamps of the Kutuk Valley in Shou Lung and the swamp Shibinuma in Kozakura. They could also be found in the many swamps of Maztica, and the island of Djinni's Claws.

Beyond Toril, giant leeches inhabited the marshes and swamps of the planet Oerth.  Beyond the Prime Material plane, giant leeches could be found in the Outlands in the marsh that surrounded the gate-town of Torch.

Relationships
Giant snapping turtles and giant gars were two of many animals known to prey upon giant leeches. Of more intelligent creatures, kobolds were occasionally known to hunt down and roast them on a spit like one would a lamb. Some claimed that bullywugs within the Mere of Dead Men would use them as mounts.

Prior to Mintar's takeover by worshipers of Bane, the hunting of these creatures was a favorite sport among nobility. They would lure them to the surface with blood or raw meat, then either the noble or a hired leech-hunter would spear them with small, barbed harpoons. Those who brought in the largest of leeches during any of the city-state's several fishing festivals would earn much accord.

Some swamp-dwelling creatures would use spider webs to help bites from giant leeches to clot faster.

Some nereids were known to keep giant leeches as pets.

Usage
Some creatures would hunt down giant leeches, seeking to make poisons from their saliva. Others used them as containers for blood, since the substance didn't coagulate inside their digestive trat.

Summoning
A giant leech could be summoned by the summon monster I spell.

Appendix
Notes

Appearances

References

## Giant porcupine

Giant porcupines were a larger than normal species of the rodents known as porcupine.

Description
Giant porcupines were largely identical to the mundane, brown variety of porcupine, though their quills was far more noticeable. Quills were stiff, sharp hairs that lined their backs. Their bodies were covered in around three-hundred quills, with their tails in particular having around eighty. Each of these quills measured  in length. Their paws made them very skilled at climbing trees.

Behavior
These creatures had only a base, animal level of intelligence, but were relatively non-aggressive unless threatened. Being very timid and prone to panic, they tended to view creatures within  of them as potential threats. Whenever a creature came within that range, giant porcupines would crouch down in a defensive posture and utter a warning hiss. If the creature continued to approach them, they were liable to either run away or defend themselves.

Combat
Much like their smaller cousins, giant porcupines defended themselves by launching the quills from their tails at enemies like projectiles. In dire situations they would resort to even biting an opponent. They were capable of shooting their quills at enemies from up to  away.

Ecology
Much like their smaller cousins, these creatures lived in family units. Though willing to socialize with others, they preferred remaining with their family unit.
Diet
Giant porcupines were a herbivorous species. They largely ate fruit, tree bark, and tree leaves. They were also very fond of salt.
Habitats
Giant porcupines were primarily encountered in wooded areas, particularly those in temperate climates. For example, in Maztica they were known to be encountered all across its jungles. In Faerûn they could be found in the forest of Starwood and Semberholme.

They could also be found in the Goblin Marches and Stonelands, being one the few monstrously sized species that remained following the goblinoids' settlement of the area.

In the region of Anauroch they could be found in the fertile areas of the Plain of Standing Stones.

Outside of the Prime Material plane, giant porcupines inhabited the Celestial Bureaucracy's plane of Qui.
Relationships
Giant porcupines had very few predators.

Those that lived in the Plain of Standing Stones were occasionally hunted by hill giants.
Usage
Much like their smaller cousins, in the 14 century DR there were no known potion or spell applications for the material components that could be harvested from giant porcupines.

Appendix
Appearances
References

---

## Giant Portuguese man-o-war

The giant Portuguese man-o-war was a species of monstrously sized aquatic creature, a sort of giant jellyfish.

Description
These creatures had largely transparent bodies, which were difficult for most creatures to detect unless they could perceive invisible objects. They typically sported around ten to forty tentacles, with their length varying depending upon the specimen's size.

The smallest specimens were  in diameter and typically had ten tentacles that each measured  in length. The largest specimens were  in diameter and typically had forty tentacles that each measured  in length.

Abilities
The tentacles of these creatures were coated in a paralytic poison. They used this to stun their prey for easier meals. When tentacles were sliced off it did no harm to the central body of a giant Portuguese man-o-war and the tentacles would regenerate over the course of several days.

Ecology
Giant Portuguese men-o-war were known to live either alone or in groups of up to ten members, though they primarily lived solitarily.

Habitats
These creatures were primarily found in warm, tropical oceans, in shallows. They were rarely found deeper than, the range in which light could easily penetrate the water. They often washed up on beaches during storms, where their tentacles became partially buried.

In the Realms, they were only known to inhabit the seas and ocean around the Moonshae Isles.
Relationships
When beached by storms, sea birds and crustaceans would prey upon their helpless bodies.

Usage
Some sahuagin would place these creatures within their lairs to act as traps against any would-be intruders.

History
Around the 12 century DR, the famed pirate captain Immurk and his men transported two giant Portuguese man-o-wars to an underwater grotto in the Egg of the Isles, a small islet in the Pirate Isles, to act as guardians of his treasure.

Appendix
Appearances
Adventures
Dungeon #48, "The Oracle at Sumbar"
References

---

## Giant otter

Giant otters were a monstrous variety of the mundane aquatic mammal of the same name.

Description
Giant otters were both fast runners and fast swimmers. Their bodies measured  in length.

Behavior
Much like the mundane otter, these creatures were relatively peaceful and playful creatures. Sliding around and playing tag were some of their favorite past times.

Giant otters would not attack others unless threatened, cornered, or their young were threatened. In which case, they fought fiercely. Even their young would fight to defend themselves.

Ecology
Diet
Giant crayfish were one of many creatures they were known to prey upon.
Habitats
Much like their smaller kin, giant otters could be found inhabiting a variety of lakes and rivers. They were particularly known to inhabit the Lake of Steam in South Faerûn, the Moonsea lake, freshwater bodies in the Moonshae Isles, as well as the rivers and coasts of Phlan.

Beyond the Prime Material plane, they could be found in the River Oceanus.

Relationships
Giant otters tended to cause some collateral damage as they played. Breaking carts, overturning boats or wagons, and panicking horses.

Some nereids were known to keep giant otters as pets. And some aquatic rangers were known to have them as animal companions.

Summoning
Those who specialized in sea-based magic could summon between 1-3 giant otters with the monster summoning IV spell.A giant otter could also be summoned by means of the conjure animals spell.

Usages
Much like the mundane otter, creatures were known to hunt this giant variety for their pelts. Their pelts typically sold for around 1,000 to 4,000 gold pieces.

Adventures
Ruins of Adventure
Organized Play & Licensed Adventures
A Fool's Errand

## Giant pike

Giant pikes were a monstrous variety of the mundane fish known as pikes.

Description
Giant pikes had long, flexible bodies with greenish-yellow scales that helped them blend into parts of their environment. Their bodies could grow up to  in length. They were also very rapid swimmers.

Behavior
Giant pikes were very aggressive. When hungry, they were liable to attack almost any nearby creature.

Biology
These fish possessed very keen eyesight.

Combat
Though giant pikes sometimes stalked their prey, they typically hunted by waiting in some seaweed for potential prey to swim by. Due to their greenish-yellow scales their bodies were camouflaged within the underwater plant life. Due to this and their rapid speed of movement, giant pikes often surprised their victims.

Ecology
Diet
Giant pikes were a carnivorous, predatory species.

Habitats
These creatures were typically found inside large, deep lakes of fresh water in temperate regions.

In Faerûn, they inhabited some of the bodies of water across Cormanthor, particularly the River Ashaba. Deep within the Sea of Fallen Stars, they were a relatively common variety of giant fish around Serôs.

On the Moonshae Isles they could be found in various bodies of freshwater. In the Unapproachable East, giant pikes could be found in the lakes of Rashemen.

In the Underdark of the Bloodstone Lands, they could be found in the upper lake of Deepearth.

Beyond Faerûn, they could be found in the tropical and subtropical fresh waters of Kara-Tur. During the summer season they could be found in the lakes, rivers, and swamps of the Ama Basin. Beyond the Prime Material plane, they inhabited the Celestial Bureaucracy's plane of Qui. They could also be found in some parts of the River Styx.

Relationships
Nixies were often known to domesticate giant pikes.

Some placed giant pikes within their moats to act as guard animals.

Appendix
Notes

Appearances

References

## Giant sea turtle

Giant sea turtles, otherwise known simply as giant turtles, were a monstrous variety of the mundane reptiles of the same name.

Description
These creatures resembled the mundane sea turtle in all ways but size, including being very slow-moving. They had large bony shells — the upper half known as a carapace and the lower half known as a plaston — from which their limbs and head extended out from. While some species could hide themselves within these shells, others were incapable of doing so and thus could only plaster their limbs to the sides of their shells for protection.

Behavior
These creatures were largely non-aggressive. They usually tended to hide within their shells when confronted with danger rather than, rather than fleeing or fighting. Though if seriously harmed, persistently abused, or persistently annoyed they would fight fiercely.

Biology
Giant turtles had very long lifespans, ranging from thirty to up to a hundred and fifty years.

Combat
These creatures fought by using their giant jaws.

Species
Archelon ischyras An ancient, prehistoric species of giant turtle that in some places persisted well beyond the Days of Thunder.
Giant snapping turtle A monstrous variety of snapping turtle. They were commonly found in large lakes or rivers. Though there also existed a subspecies unique to Zakhara, that was adapted to living on land in the High Desert.
Zaratan A gargantuan species of giant turtle that were often mistaken for islands. They primarily inhabited the seas around Zakhara.

Reputation
Small boats were often capsized due to giant turtles surfacing from underneath them.

Ecology
Diet
Giant turtles were an omnivorous species and would eat whatever they could find in their habitat. This included fish, small mammals, insects like worms, and fresh green vegetation. They had a distaste for carrion and rotting vegetation, but would resort to consuming them if starving.
Habitats
Giant turtles could be found in a variety of oceans and seas. On Toril they were particularly found in the waters around the Moonshae Isles, Halruaa, the Sarradra river in Neth Stand, as well as the rivers and coasts of Phlan.

Beyond Faerûn, giant sea turtles could be found around the island chain of Nada al-Hazan in the Crowded Sea.

Beyond Toril, these creatures could be found on the planet of Oerth.

Usage
The meat of these creatures was considered a delicacy by many cultures, being very palatable and highly nourishing. With those that dwelled in tropical regions being the most delicious.

Some were known to transform the harvested carapaces of giant turtles into small huts, strong roofs, or even boats. Those that dwelled in tropical regions were said to have the most attractive of shells. The people of Halruua were known to fashion the shells of giant turtles into polished, protective plates for the hulls of their skyships.

Rumors & Legends
Some claimed that there existed a species of giant turtle similar to the giant snapping turtle, but adapted to arid desert regions.

Trivia
 The Bone Throne within the Dungeon Level of Undermountain was partially made from the plaston (under carapace) of a giant turtle.
 Those who specialized in sea-based magic could summon a giant sea turtle with the spell monster summoning VIII.

 Dragon turtle
 Lung wang

## Giant lamprey

Giant lampreys, also called Sea lampreys were a monstrous variety of the parasitic leech-like eels of the same name.

Description
Much like the mundane lamprey, these creatures had round sphincter-like orifices that were ringed with sharp teeth. Their bodies measured around  in length. They typically had a sickly brown, green, or grayish coloring.

Behavior
These creatures had horrible tempers. They were largely driven by a desire to eat and reproduce. They tended to aggressively fight other creatures in order to protect their lairs.

Abilities
Biology
Much like their smaller kin, giant lamprey had a near flawless homing sense, allowing them to pinpoint creatures up to  away. They also were particularly vulnerable to fire.

These creatures injected some form of poison into the creatures that they attached themselves. While this poison was harmless to creatures other than fish, it imparted a severely sour taste upon the latter that many considered to be inedible.

Combat
Much like a leech or giant leech, used their mouths to fasten themselves to prey, from which they gradually drained blood. Once full, they detached themselves from their prey, but otherwise they largely refused to let go unless killed.

Ecology
Habitats
Giant lampreys could be found in both fresh and saltwater environments, but were almost never encountered in shallow water, only deep waters. They typically made their lairs within underwater caves or coral formations.

In Faerun, they were particularly known to the inhabit the lake Moonsea, the River Ashaba of Cormanthor, Dragon Reach, and the Sea of Fallen Stars. On the Moonshae Isles, they could be found in various bodies of freshwater. In the Unapproachable East, giant lamprey could be found in the lakes of Rashemen.

Relationships
In salt water habitats, sharks often preyed upon giant lamprey.

Summoning
A sea lamprey could be summoned by the summon monster V spell in saltwater environments. Those who specialized in sea-based magic could summon up to 2 sea lampreys with the same monster summoning V spell.

Rumors & Legends
 Local legends around Marsember persistently claimed that obscure sea cults dwelled in the city that fed living sacrifices to giant lamprey.

## Giant stag beetle

Giant stag beetles were a species of giant beetle and a monstrous variety of the species known as stag beetles.

Description
The bodies of these creatures typically grew to be  in length. They had heavily armored exoskeletons and two horn-like mandibles, which could grow to be  in length at the least.

Behavior
Giant stag beetles were sometimes repelled by fire, though this was not a guaranteed method to ward them off. When engaging in combat they almost never tried to retreat.

Abilities
Much like other species of beetle, the giant stag had very a poor sense of hearing and eyesight. Because of this, they primarily relied upon their tactile and taste senses. The latter was done by means of their antennae, which also contained olfactory organs.

Combat
Giant stag beetles were known to often attack something organic if their senses considered edible. They also would always retaliate to being attacked. When fighting other creatures, giant stag beetles would often try to trample them. Whenever possible they tried to attack from behind.

History
In 1372 DR, a giant stag beetle was one of the creatures that the animal keeper Ferdinand kept in his menagerie at Neverwinter Academy.

Ecology
Those that inhabited the Dalelands were known to form small colonies of four to eight mature members. One beetle was always assigned the duty of guarding the lair and would touch antennae with other giant stag beetles in order to ascertain whether they were part of the same colony or not.
Diet
Giant stag beetles were a herbivorous species. They had a great fondness for grain and similar cultivated crops. They would sometimes raid farmlands in order to obtain them.

Lairs
The lairs of these beetles were fortified and had entrances similar to a gopher hole, though resembling more of a flattened oval that was as long as it was wide. These entrances were only big enough for mature giant stag beetles to squeeze through. There was then a second opening that was used as an escape route in case predators entered.

Giant stag beetles would construct their lairs near trees infested with stirges when possible, as they would leave the beetles alone and fend off any intrusive mammals. Similarly, they allowed predators that had no taste for them to cohabitate in these lairs, such as fire lizards and giant badgers.

Their lairs always featured two nests, with one being a decoy. Their true nests were covered with a foot of leaves and sticks., while the latter ere made from rotting leaves and small sticks. Females would chew these leaves in their mandibles, spreading a colorless and odorless acid upon them.
Habitats
Giant stag beetles were generally encountered in forests of regions with a temperate climate.

In North Faerûn they were known to inhabit the forests of Cormanthor, particularly in the Midwood area. In the forests of and near the cultivated areas of Cormyr, Sembia, and the Dalelands. Particularly in Battledale, Mistledale, and Shadowdale.

In south Faerûn's Shining South, they could be found in the Forest of Amtar and the Great Rift.

In the Unapproachable East, giant stag beetles could be found in the Forest of Lethyr, Rawlinswood, and Yuirwood. In the Hordelands, they could be found in the southern forest of Shalhoond.

On the Chultan Peninsula, giant stag beetles were a common sight in the jungles of the nation of Samarach.

Beyond Toril, they could be found on the planet of Krynn.

Relationships
Gargantuan praying mantises were one of the main creatures to prey upon giant stag beetles.

Due to their dietary habits many creatures who cultivated crops considered these beetles to be a serious pest, as they would greedily devour an entire acre of crops if given the opportunity. Farmers in the Dalelands considered them to be a greater threat to their crops than disease or poor weather.

Trivia
 A giant stag beetle could be summoned by an amber amulet of vermin.
 The best known method for exterminating these creatures was a concoction known as stag beetle poison.

## Giant water beetle

Giant water beetles, otherwise known simply as water beetles, were a species of giant beetle.

Description
The bodies of these beetles typically grew to be  in length. They were rapid swimmers, but on land they moved at a rather slow and ponderous pace.

Abilities
Like most species of beetle, these creatures lacked good eyesight or hearing. Because of this, they primarily relied upon their tactile and taste senses. The latter was done by means of their antennae. They also hunted by sensing vibrations in the water.

Giant water beetles were air-breathing creatures, though they could remain underwater for extended periods of time. This was done by catching and trapping air bubbles beneath their large wings. They often carried these air bubbles to some underwater cave or cavity capable of holding an air supply.

Ecology
Giant water beetles largely lived solitarily lives.
Diet
These beetles were a voracious species of omnivores. Like other species of beetle they were liable to eat almost any form of organic matter, though they preferred to prey upon almost any form of animal. They would often attack and sink shipping vessels in order to get at the food inside.
Habitats
Giant water beetles were typically found inhabiting bodies of fresh water, no less than  deep. With these sometimes being navigable rivers or lakes.

They were known to be found in some of the moors and rivers of the North, occasionally in Anauroch in the River of Gems, and in the temperate areas of the Sea of Fallen Stars.

In the Hordelands they could be found in the lakes, rivers, and swamps of the Ama Basin during the summer season. In neighboring continent of Kara-tur they could be found in temperate, tropical, and sub-tropical bodies of water, as well as in temperate swamps. Such as the rivers of Akari island.

They could also be found in dungeons, such as in the Wyllowwood level of Undermountain.

History
Around the, the Flotilla of DEATH chased a lost giant water beetle away from the city of Ravens Bluff.Port of Ravens Bluff (pp. 42, 45) states that Charles O'Kane became Mayor 20 years ago, which would set it in 1361 DR.

Trivia
A giant water beetle could be summoned by the summon monster IV spell if the caster was upon a body of water or underwater.

## Giant elk

Giant elks, otherwise known as giant stags, were a rare species of larger than usual deer.

Description
On average these creatures stood  tall at the shoulder and weighed around .

Combat
Giant elks fought other creatures with either their antlers or their sharp forehooves.

Ecology
Habitats
Like any species of deer, giant elks were typically found in forests with a temperate climate.

In the Border Kingdoms they could be found in the forest of Hawkgarth. In the region of Kara-tur, they could be found in the forests of Akari island.

Relationships
Many cultures considered it taboo to hunt these creatures. They were often preyed upon by dire wolves and saber-toothed tigers.

Rumors & Legends
These creatures were so rare that sightings were interpreted as foreshadowing some important event, like the birth of a future king. Some legends even spoke of deities manifesting on the Prime Material plane in the form of giant elks.

## Giant sheep

Giant sheep, also known as giant rams in the case of males, were a rare species of larger than usual sheep.

Description
The average giant sheep, regardless of sex, grew to be around  tall at the shoulder.

Behavior
Much like their smaller cousins, giant sheep were largely non-aggressive creatures. However, if they believed their flock was threatened, they would fight the perceived threat to the death.

Combat
Giant rams charged at their enemies, ramming them with their horns.

Ecology
Giant sheep lived in flocks. At the very least, these consisted of one ram, four ewes, and a number of lambs.

Habitats
These creatures primarily inhabited hilly or mountainous regions.

In the Realms they could be particularly found inhabiting the Omlarandin Mountains, east of Saradush, as well as in the highlands and coniferous forests of the Moonshae Isles.

Relationships
Some cyclopes were known to herd giant sheep. In the Omlarandin Mountains, some mountain giants were also known to herd them.

Giant sheep were a favored animal of the halfling deity Yondalla.

Usage
The horn of a giant ram could be used as a material component for the spell horn of plenty.

Trivia
A giant ram could be summoned by the conjure animals spell.

## Giant goat

Giant goats were a rare, larger than usual species of goat.
Description
On average a giant goat grew to be  tall at the shoulder. They grew long, sharp horns.

Combat
When confronting opponents, giant goats mimicked their smaller kin by charging and ramming with their horns.
Ecology
Giant goats were rather reclusive.
Diet
Giant goats were a herbivorous species.
Habitats
These creatures were typically found in hilly regions.

Giant goats were known to inhabit the Stonelands, Hartsvale, and Deadstone Cleft.
Relationships
Some creatures were known to domesticate and herd giant goats, such as stone giants.

Occasionally giant goats were used as mounts, especially by druids that frequented mountainous regions.

Trivia
A giant goat could be summoned by the conjure animals spell.

## Giant troll

Giant trolls were a rare subrace of trolls that resulted from the crossbreeding of trolls and hill giants.

Description
These creatures largely resembled trolls, albeit much larger and with the pot-bellied build typical of hill giants. Their heads bore a closer resemblance to hill giants and they lacked the razor-sharp teeth typical of trolls. Their skin was a reddish-brown hue and their eyes had red rims.

Behavior
Much like both of the races they descended from, these creatures on average possessed a low intelligence.

Abilities
Giant trolls had a very keen sense of smell and their eyes were capable of seeing in total darkness out to a range of .

Giant trolls possessed the regenerative ability common among trolls and needed to be hurt with fire in order to be fully killed. However, they were unable to reattach severed limbs.

Combat
These creatures typically attacked with large, spiked clubs, made from tree trunks or the timber of a house.  When such a thing was not possible, they might resort to their clawed hands. However, when unarmed a giant troll generally preferred to grasp smaller opponents in their claws and wield them like a club. When overwhelmed by smaller creatures, they were likely to grab and and hurl those they discern as warriors at trees to give them time to deal with the weaker creatures.

Unlike other trolls, giant trolls were never known to fight by biting.

History
In 1358 DR, giant trolls were part of the Muktaran empire's forces, comprising a unit known as Baz's Bashers that was led by a giant troll of the same name.

Around 1368 DR, a number of giant trolls were among the forces led by the troll TorGal in his siege of Arnise Hold. Gorion's Ward faced two of them within its courtyard, than faced another pair within its cellar that were accompanying TorGal. Later that same year,The first date in the in-game journal is Uktar 12, 1368 DR. Gorion's Ward confronted giant trolls while making their way through the Troll Claw Woods.

Society
Giant trolls were rarely ever encountered alone, typically living and traveling in tribal bands.
Homelands
Giant trolls were relatively adaptable, being found in any type of climate and any type of terrain.

In Faerûn they were primarily known to be found in the North, particularly in the Trollmoors and the Hall of Four Ghosts. In the Western Heartlands they could be found in the Trollclaws.
Relationships
The standard variety of troll typically served these creatures in their lairs, acting as lackeys and sometimes food. They tended to be on good terms with hill giant tribes, serving as elite personal guards for their chiefs.
Religions
Some giant trolls took up the life of a shaman of Vaprak.

Notable Giant Trolls
 TorGal, leader of the Rocksmash pack of trolls.
 Two giant trolls from Prime Material could be found on Phlegethon, wandering in caverns near a town called Windglum, having been shifted to the plane by some prime wizard.

Notes

 Giant two-headed troll, another subrace of trolls that resulted from crossbreeding with giantkin.

## Giant firefly

Giant fireflies, also known as firefriends, were a rarely encountered much larger relative of the mundane firefly.

Description
These creatures had a pair of sharp mandibles and a phosphorescent abdomen that had similar levels of luminosity to a torch. This light was of a soft green or yellow hue and was shed within a  radius. Their bodies could grow to be up to  in length, weigh little more than, and were brightly colored. Usually ranging from emerald green to metallic blue. Their wings could on average grow to a span of  and contained a large number of blood vessels.

Behavior
Giant fireflies had a level of intellect slightly above that of most insects, though still relatively low. They were quite clever, had friendly dispositions, and shunned obvious threats. They typically only attacked if provoked.

Abilities
These creatures possessed some form of natural resistance against fire and lightning. In addition, they were capable of adjusting the brightness of their abdomen and concentrating it, shooting out a greenish beam of light that was  in diameter, up to a length of . In addition, they could keep their abdomen's light glowing for indefinitely for a short period.

The eyes of giant fireflies were capable of seeing in areas of total darkness.
Carrying Capacity
The largest specimens of this species were capable of carrying various loads of equipment. For them a small load was roughly equal to and below, a medium load capped off at, and the heaviest load they could manage was .

Combat
Outside of the concentrated beam of light from their abdomens, giant fireflies were relatively poor combatants.

Ecology
These creatures hatched in the spring and reached maturity by the age of two. Overall they had a lifespan of ten or so years. They used their superior flying skills in intricate aerial courtship battles and dances, which could last for hours.
Diet
These were a carnivorous species, feeding upon smaller insects and even birds.
Habitats
Giant fireflies preferred warn and wet environments, such as tropical forests and marshes. They were integral to the ecosystems of many tropical areas. In their larval stage they were largely aquatic creatures.
Languages
In addition to their own insectoid language, these creatures were often capable of speaking Common. They typically learned how to say the latter by the age of one or two.
Relationships
Giant fireflies often sought humans and demihumans as companions, leading many to hold them and high regard and their alternative name, "firefriends." They loved to converse with such creatures and hear stories of a fanciful nature. Some giant fireflies grew so large that other creatures used them as flying mounts, sometimes using them for the purpose of search and rescue. Riding one of these creatures required a special saddle

These insects were a favored animal of the deities Brandobaris and Sheela Peryroyl.
Usages
The egg of a giant firefly could fetch prices of up to 300 gold pieces, while professional trainers fetched a similar price.

## Giant spitting snake

Giant spitting snakes, also known simply as spitting snakes, were a rare variety of giant snake.

Behavior
Like any snake they feared fire and would retreat from open flames. They had a basic level of intelligence typical of animals.

Abilities
These creatures had a poisonous bite and as their name implied they were capable of projecting it, spraying their spittle up at creatures within  of them.

Sub-Species
Sea-stings An aquatic species of spitting snake that inhabited areas with cold water. They were known to be found around some areas of the Moonshae Isles.

Ecology
Like all giant snakes, these creatures lived solitary lives.

Diet
Like all species of snake, these creatures were carnivorous.

Habitats
Giant spitting snakes inhabited almost any type of terrain in regions with a tropical climate. In the Realms, these creatures could be found in the deserts of Anauroch, and the low hills of the Storm Horns region.

In Kara-tur, they could be found in that region's tropical and subtropical deserts, its tropical and subtropical swamps, as well as in the forests of Akari island

## Ginny Tollerson

Ginny Tollerson was a happy human citizen of Daggerford who loved the city so much she took on herself the role of an unofficial one-woman-tourist center for the visitors and new arrivals circa 1372 DR.

Personality
Ginny Tollerson was a happy-go-lucky woman who gladly chatted up new arrivals to Daggerford and eagerly dispensed directions and information about her city.

She did not like the fancy inn called Chateau Elite as it was too upper-crusty for her taste.

History
Ginny Tollerson was born in Daggerford and happily lived her whole life there.

In 1372 DR, Ginny greeted the newly arrived adventurers, soon to become the heroes of Daggerford. She directed them to the city's two temples, the marketplace, and recommended the Happy Cow inn over the shady and unruly Black Stone Inn that even the city guards stopped keeping tabs on the seedy establishment and its owner Gildamesh.

History
The Sword Coast Legion was a military cleric organization that was outfitted by the girdles of holy might to aid in their fight against ravaging orc gangs in the, the Year of the Black Horde.

## Gigwhorl

A gigwhorl was a small device used to create distracting sounds.

Uses
Originating in Calimshan, these tightly wound tops had a locking spring mechanism that would unwind when the gigwhorl was tossed, creating a loud clattering noise when they struck the ground. This device was useful to distract guards or to create noise to cover other sound.

A gigwhorl cost 3 gold pieces through Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue.
