## Giant scarab beetle

Giant scarab beetles were reclusive dung beetles and enemies of the undead, found in Underdark and Undermountain.

Description
Giant scarab beetles, like their mundane cousins, were round insects with black or brown carapace. Some rarer species had a bright blue shell with gem-like red spots, and each piece of carapace - countered in gold.

Behavior
Giant scarab beetles could often be found in mated pairs or small groups, no bigger than four insects. Being reclusive creatures, they preferred to stay out of combat with predators. They often could be found in bigger-sized caverns, rolling large balls of dung, or hunting small critters.

Giant scarabs had vestigial wings, well protected by their carapace. The wings allowed them to fly short distances, or, to be more precise, to jump and hover as far as . When the beetles used the wings, they generated loud clacking and buzzing sounds.

Combat

When attacked, the giant scarabs attempted to flee most of the time, jumping away from danger. Their main defensive ability was creating a magical flare from under their wings. The bright light had a radius of  and dispersed any and all magical shadows or darkness within  from their bodies. The same magical light damaged undead, shadows, shadow fiends, slow shadows, darkness elementals, and similar creatures. This magical flare could last up to six minutes, burning the affected creatures continuously. When lit, the giant scarabs were unable to fly. Additionally, giant scarabs used their mandibles to deliver biting attacks.

Ecology
Giant scarab beetles were omnivores and not picky eaters. In Underdark, they often fed on bat guano, various insects like cave crickets. Scarabs considered all smaller creatures to be their food source. When they came across undead and successfully laid the creatures, the scarabs fed on their remains. These insects could consume most undead creatures, from simple skeletons to vampires.

In the wild, giant scarabs were natural enemies of the undead and kept their populations in check in the vicinity of their habitats. Many worshipers of the Mulhorandi pantheon believed that these creatures were created by Re, the God of Sun, due to the beetles' instinct to swarm the undead opponents, burning them away with their magical flare light.

Habitat
Giant scarab beetles could bee found in Underdark, Undermountain, and subterranean locations in Mulhorand. They preferred to nest in underground areas with abundant dung. The nests were simple round burrowed tunnels approximately  in diameter, allowing the beetles to move along, but nothing bigger could squeeze through, keeping them safe from predators. Giant scarabs were dung beetles and kept balls of it tucked away inside their nests, reeking up their tunnels. The dung balls were used to incurable the creatures' eggs, deposited inside. Their lairs were usually pretty dry and, on occasion, had stray treasure embedded into the tunnels' walls.

Mulhorandi used beautifully colored blue carapace giant scarabs to guard their tombs.

Outside of the Prime Material plane, giant scarab beetles could be encountered on Thanatos, 113 layer of the Abyss, in Naratyr. There they often were kept as guard pets. They could also be found in the Domains of Dread.

Cultural Significance
Mulhorandi considered scarabs, giant and regular, to be holy animals of Re, by various cults worshiping sun gods. These insects symbolized rebirth.

History
In 1370 DR, an agent of Khelben Arunsun encountered giant scarabs guarding the Tomb of Ankh-Kephra in Undermountain. The tomb was a home for Mulhorandi wizards Ankh-Kephra and Nephryt. The wizards constructed the tomb to spend their eternal undeath together. The wizards build the tomb in accordance with the designs and traditions of their homeland. One of the tomb's many protectors were beautifully hued guard giant blue scarabs from Mulhorand.

## Giant clam

Giant clams were a large variety of the creatures of the same name, also including oysters and scallops.

Description

These were soft-bodied creatures that lived in the mantle cavity of protective shells. The abductor muscles of these creatures acted as a hinge for these shells and were quite powerful. Lining the lips of their shell were numerous, hair-like cilia. These were small tentacles about  in length, which they used to grasp and move prey. These were too weak to act in any defensive manner. And they used inhalant and exhalant siphons to move these shells across the ocean floor.

The protective shell of a giant clam was vertically symmetrical and on average were around  in diameter, though both smaller and larger specimens could be found. Their upper-shells were typically of a light brown hue, occasionally with white markings, while the lower-shell would be of a white hue. Near the edge of a shell were tiny blue eye-spots which performed the creature's senses. The inside of these shells were lined with mother of pearl. Some even held giant-sized pearls, generally around the size of a grapefruit, though these were not as lustrous as the pearls of mundane clams.

Some species of giant clam had wavy-edged shells that were so sharp they could potentially sever a limb. Those known as giant oysters had a more elongated and uneven shape, as well as a grayish hue.

Abilities
The eye-spots of these creatures could detect movement as well as distinguish between light and shadow. However, they could not estimate size. In effect, they possessed a form of tremorsense.

Near the front of a giant clam's mantle cavity were special organs that could detect and analyze chemical traces within the water around them.

Combat

Whenever a giant clam was threatened or something tried to reach within it, their protective shell would clamp down. This was an instinctive defense mechanism, caused by their cilia being brushed against. Many naturalists in the Realms insisted that these could not close fast enough to cause any serious injury.

Ecology
These creatures lived within small groups, though there existed persistent rumors that large beds of them could be found.
Diet
Giant clams were primarily a filter feeding species, eating microscopic sea animals such as shrimp. They also lived on algae colonies that grew within their shell mantle.
Habitats
These creatures were typically found in shallow ocean waters, at depths of up to, often in the vicinity of hydrothermal vents. They were particularly known to be found within the Sea of Fallen Stars and the connected River Lapendrar, within the oyster reef section of the Sea of Moving Ice, and within the Trackless Sea. In the Trackless Sea, many giant clams could be found throughout the stronghold Maelstrom.

Beyond Faerûn, they could be found in parts of the Crowded Sea, such as the bay of Batihat al-Saji.

In the Elemental Plane of Water, giant clams could be found around the coral reef compound known as the Citadel of Ten Thousand Pearls.

Languages
Communication with these creatures was not possible except on the most basic of empathic levels.
Relationships
Tritons were known to have domesticated giant clams and tended to beds of them. Some giant clams acted as companions or servants of noble marids. And giant starfish sometimes preyed upon giant clams.

Usages

The malenti colony of Kymal used giant clam shells as part of constructing their residences. Likewise, some octopi were known to lair within giant clams.

The shalarin of the Sea of Fallen Stars would carefully insert clothing constructed from sheeps' coral into the maws of giant clams, leaving them there for two months to be coated in their mucous. The shalarin then tempered that mucus with special shells, turning their clothing into pearl armor.

Their tongues could be used as an ingredient for potions of giant size. Their pearls could be used to create the soundbox of an 'Ud of the marids.

The mother of pearl within their shells were worth around 50 to 500 gold pieces. And depending on their size and quality, a giant pearl could be worth around 500 to 1,000 gold pieces. The rare black pearl of a giant clam was worth around 5,000 gold pieces.

Rumors & Legends
 Some rumors claimed that there existed intelligent, or even psionic, colonies of giant clams.
 Local legends around Marsember persistently claimed that obscure sea cults dwelled in the city that fed living sacrifices to giant clams.

Trivia
 The field manuals of some navies in the Realms described methods of how one could escape from the clamped maw of a giant clam.
 The blue dragon Iymrith sometimes used her magic to transform her foes into giant clams.
 The vampire spawn merman Arrick Kaarvol used a giant clam as a coffin.

## Giant lobster

Giant lobsters were a monstrous variety of the crustaceans of the same name.These core sources — Monstrous Manual on page 50, Monstrous Compendium Volume Two on page 29, and the 1 edition Dungeon Masters Guide on pages 180 & 224 — all state that crayfish and lobsters are the same thing. While crayfish are similar to lobsters and are even colloquially known as "freshwater lobsters," they are distinct creatures.

History
Around the 14 century DR, a juvenile storm giant named Alexandra went into the Dragon Reach with an apparatus of Kwalish that had been gifted to her by her father and playfully interacted with some of the local giant lobsters.

Around that time, one could purchase a whole giant lobster to dine upon at Sharkey's Bar & Grill.

Ecology
Habitats
These creatures were known to inhabit the Dragon Reach.
Relationships
Giant nautiluses were known to prey upon these creatures.
Summoning
A giant lobster could be summoned with the spell Summon monster IV.

## Giant toad

Giant toads were monstrous varieties of the mundane animals of the same name.

Description
In terms of physical description, giant toads completely matched normal toads, except in size. Their skin was dry and bumpy or "warty" as some described it, often rough to the touch. However, their appearance varied between species. The base species of giant toad has colorations ranging from weak brown to iron red.

On average the base species of giant toad grew up to be  in length.

Abilities
Due to their extended size, a giant toad could jump up to  high, and across gaps that were  wide, even from being completely stationary.

Sub-Species

Fire toad
 A variety of giant toad that was capable of launching fireballs from its mouth. Unlike other giant toad species, they had hydrophobia rather than a fear of fire.
Ice toad
 A highly intelligent variety of giant toad that were adapted for life in cold habitats.
Poisonous toad
 A variety of giant toad that largely looked the same as the mundane variety. However, their bodies secreted a type of poison through their skin. They were less common than the standard giant toad.

Ecology
Diet
Giant toads were a carnivorous species, liable to attempt to devour anything that appeared edible. They were often attracted to settled areas, where they preyed upon livestock such as chickens, goats, and even sheep.
Habitats
Giant toads preferred to live in temperate areas with bodies of water they could lay their eggs in. Though they were species that survived in rather cool or dry environments.

They often made their homes underground, enjoying both the damp air and steady supply of food such environments offered them. Because of this, they occasionally be found within the sewers of cities.

In northwest Faerûn, giant toads could be found within the Mere of Dead Men. In interior Faerûn they could be found in the Farsea Marshes, the Marsh of Tun, and the Vast Swamp. In southeast Faerûn they could be found in the Great Swamp of Rethild.

Beyond Faerûn, they could be found in the prairie regions of Semphar.

## Gideon Lightward

Gideon Lightward was a corrupted priest of Lathander in Elturel during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
Despite being corrupted, Gideon still viewed himself as a bastion of good because his objective was only to destroy all demons. However he was filled with hatred for those that did not obey his commands.

History
Gideon was a very zealous priest of Lathander. Zariel, believing him a very useful asset, in the  sent various devils posing as angels to slowly corrupt him by offering great power to battle demons.

In time, Gideon gave his free will completely to the archdevil and when Elturel was dragged down to the Nine Hells he was Zariel's most loyal servant. Gideon died during the fall but rose as an undead creature.

Thanks to the dark energies of the Companion, he began to assemble all the dead of the city's cemetery into a undead army to battle the demons.

He barricaded himself inside the Chapel of Mourning together with two mezzoloths while waiting to resurrect his army of undead.

## Giant crane

Giant cranes were a monstrously sized species of crane.

Behavior
These creatures had an average animal degree of intelligence. Though generally non-aggressive, giant cranes were very protective of their mates, young, and nesting areas.

Ecology
Diet
These creatures were a carnivorous species, preying upon large fish.
Habitats
Giant cranes were generally found in the same habitats of similar wading birds, such as ordinary cranes, herons, and bitterns. These habitats included marshes, swamps, lake verges, and along the banks of watercourses.

Giant cranes could be found in Kara-Tur's temperate plains, as well as in its tropical and sub-tropical swamps.

## Gideos

Gideos was the mind flayer leader of the Bloodrovers, a pirate group that operated in Avernus in the late 15 century DR.

Description
Gideos wore an eye patch over its left eye, which concealed a gem that, when exposed, amplified its telepathic capabilities.

Activities
The Bloodrovers, led by Gideos, roamed the blasted wasteland of Avernus in a fleet of infernal war machines in search for treasure and soul coins. The pirate band included orcs, goblins, kobolds, and other humanoids.

History
In the, Gideos witnessed the pulling of Elturel into Avernus. Seeing the event as an opportunity, it commanded the Bloodrovers to sabotage the machines that operated the chains that were pulling the city down, in an attempt to steal the stash of soul coins that fueled them.

During the attack, the pirates inadvertently captured Minsc and Nerys. As they attempted to escape and fend off the counterattacking demons, Minsc, Nerys, and the infernal war machine that had captured them fell into the Styx. The pirates rescued them, but Minsc completely lost his memory as a result of being exposed to the river's waters.

After the rescue, Gideos commanded the Bloodrovers to enter the fortress that housed one of the chains binding Elturel. With help from Nerys and a confused Minsc, the group successfully sabotaged four of the eight chains that held the city and collected a substantial amount of soul coins. Gideos regarded the heist as the greatest in Hells' history.

## Giant sea anemone

Giant sea anemones were a monstrous species of the creatures of the same name, which many mistook for plants.

Description
These creatures had a stout central body that was  in diameter and crowned with a gaping maw that was  in diameter. Surrounding their mouths were a number of tentacles, with the average amount being around a hundred. Each tentacle was covered in hundreds of small, barbed, hook-like needles that would spring forth once a prey was in their grasp.

Anemones had very colorful bodies that displayed colors of blue, green, pink, red, orange, purple, or some combination of them.

Behavior
These creatures possessed no form of higher intelligence, having intellects below that of most animals.

Abilities
The barbed hooks that covered the tentacles of anemones would inject prey with a paralytic poison, which could be negated with the spell neutralize poison. So long as a tentacle was attached to a creature, the poison would continue to advance.

Any tentacle that was severed from an anemone's body would regrow at a rate of  per day. The base of the creature however, could not regenerate.

Combat
The tentacles of anemones would very swiftly ensnared any creatures that came within  of them and draw the prey towards their maw. Anemones typically would attempt to attach at least three tentacles to their prey. If a prey was inedible or too large, it would release them once they became paralyzed. If an anemone was full, then it would simply hold on to its prey for later.

When seriously threatened a giant sea anemone would draw their tentacles all the way back into their central body.

Species
Chest Anemone a variety of giant sea anemones that were specially cultivated by the storm giants of Maelstrom. They were around  tall and  wide. Unlike other species, these anemones were capable of surviving out of water and had hollow cavities within them. They were used by storm giants as both treasure chests and waste disposal systems.

Ecology
These were often encountered in a stationary position, but were capable of moving themselves at a very slow rate. Most rarely chose to do so, remaining in one spot for the duration of their lives.

These creatures reproduced by budding, with young growing out from their base. Once sufficiently grown it would break off and move to its own feeding ground.
Diet
Giant sea anemones were a carnivorous species.
Habitats
At depths greater than, giant sea anemones were typically found solitarily. In tidal pools and secluded shorelines, smaller varieties of them could be found in groups numbering up to fifty or even two hundred. These smaller varieties would fasten themselves to rocks.

These creatures could be found in the Sea of Fallen Stars.

Beyond Toril, they could be found in the oceans of the planet Krynn.
Relationships
Some creatures were unaffected by the paralytic poison of giant sea anemones, either from being immune to it or from having thick hides. This included certain brightly colored fish, known as clownfish, which would hide inside them from predators and feed on the scraps they left behind. Manta rays, who could often be found swimming among them. And barracudas, who preyed upon their tentacles.

Some aquatic races kept giant sea anemones as guard animals, fighting them just enough to keep them from wandering away.

## Girdle of masculinity/femininity

Girdles of masculinity/femininity were very rare, cursed magical belts that changed the wearer's sex.

Powers
Girdles of masculinity/femininity were exceedingly rare items that were often discussed and sought after for the powerful magics used to weave these belts into being. The effects of the item's curse were relatively minor, compared to other dangerous and deadly curses in existence. Once a girdle of masculinity/femininity was put on, the item's magic changed the wearer's sex and could only be removed by the remove curse spell or by a powerful cleric at a temple.

History
Circa 1318 DR, one of the girdles of masculinity/femininity was gifted by an unnamed court jester to Duke Lobelahn's lover. This gesture was poorly received, resulting in the jester's execution.

In 1360 DR, one of the earliest examples of a girdle of masculinity/feminity was found amongst the relics unearthed from Solon by the slaves of Ambuchar Devayam during their excavations. This one was referred to as a girdle of gender alteration, and unlike future girdles, concealed its effect from its wearer, to whom the effects were not apparent. It also did not operate on putting it on; it only took effect when removed.

Sometime circa 1367 DR, a halfling fighter wizard Brac Bristletoes from Beluir had his girdle of storm giant strength stolen. The halfling eventually recovered what he thought to be his belt. Unfortunately, what he found was a girdle of masculinity/femininity. Brac spent several years adventuring in a woman's body until he was able to acquire a wish spell from an archmage that reverted him back to his male form.

In 1368 DR, an ogre bandit was ambushing travelers on the Sword Coast's roads to the east from Candlekeep and north off the Friendly Arm inn. The ogre was obsessed with belts ripped off the bodies of travelers and adventurers he came across. The collection included a girdle of masculinity/femininity and Unshey's Elves' Bane magic belt.

## Giant Killer

The Giant Killer was an enchanted halfling sling effective against giants.

Powers
The Giant Killer possessed a +1 enchantment and, when wielded against giants and giant-kin, it effectively became a +4 magic weapon.

History
The first known wielder of the Giant Killer was a halfling shire-reeve warrior named Kim Marchwatch. The halfling used the sling against the giants who menaced his home. Marchwatch pelleted them with so many rocks that the giants either fell over or fled the onslaught. Subsequently, Kim was replaced as the shire-reeve and was forced on the road becoming an adventurer. He and his enchanted sling were believed to be consumed by the dreaded Fen Mists of Acallo in 760 DR.

By 1281 DR, the Giant Killer found its way into the stock of Conlan, the proprietor of Kuldahar's blacksmith shop.

## Ginger Palace

The Ginger Palace was the trade embassy of the Shou Lung empire in the Eastern Heartlands of Faerûn.

Description
The Ginger Palace boasted magnificent architecture, such as marble pathways and plastered walls that held glass windows lined with silver. The structures featured vibrant decorations made by the hands of highly-skill artisans, like wooden pillars with carvings of exotic beasts like basilisks, phoenixes, furry-tailed imp, and the heads of dragons.

Landmarks of the palace were vibrant and given extravagant names, such as the Path of Delight and the Arch of Many-Hued Scales, while buildings were given monikers related to spices, such as the Cinnamon House.

Geography
The Ginger Palace was located on the Dragon Coast, southeast of Elversult, just a short way south of the Snake Road.

Flora & Fauna
Due to the interests of the Ginger Palace's resident prince, several types of powerful reptiles could be found in the embassy's park, including snakes and monitor lizards, including thornbacks.

Government
The palace was headed by Imperial Minister of Spices Hsieh Han Liu. It was also the home to two Shou nobles sponsored by Minister Hsieh, Prince Kao Chou Tang and his wife Princess Wei Dao.

Trade
From within the palace Minister Hsieh oversaw Shou spice trade in the region. Every day, dozens of petitioners would gather at the inner courtyard of the palace hoping to gain audience with Prince Tang or Princess Wei Dao, sometimes waiting a matter of days.

Without the minister's permission, Prince Tang was also using the embassy as a headquarters for black market poison trade in the region.

Despite this bustle of activity, the embassy was considered a place of little importance to the Shou empire.

Defenses
The embassy was well-defended by stalwart Shou soldiers armored with scale mail and helmets that resembled the shells of tortoises, wielding long halberds,

History
In the, Lady Feng was abducted from the palace by the black dracolich Hethcypressarvil, and taken back to the undead beast's lair.

A short time later, the palace was infiltrated by the novice Harpers agent Ruha, who sought out the whereabouts of Lady Yanseldara, leader of Elversult. She caused a great disruption in the palace, getting captured and inadvertently creating a rift between Prince Tang and Wei Dao.

The embassy was destroyed in 1373 DR, with the structures reduced to rubble.

Notable Locations
 Garden of Flickering Tongues, a combination, park garden, and wildlife enclosure of several different biomes that housed a variety of exotic reptiles.

Notable Inhabitants
 Yu Po, scribe to Minister Hsieh.
 Yuan ti, one of the prince's guardsmen.

## Girdle of Gond

The girdle of Gond was an enchanted belt created by clerics of Gond to be used exclusively by rogues.

Powers
the girdle of Gond possessed numerous pouches that were filled with everything a thief, rogue, or trap-maker would even need, seemingly producing required tools to aid them in their trade. The belt assisted its wearer in finding traps and opening even the most complex locks.

History
This enchanted girdle was commissioned from priests of Gond by Jurmar the Calm, a Gond-worshiping burglar. He started his career as a simple locksmith but graduated to using his skills in burglary.

By 1281 DR, the girdle of Gond came to be owned by Orrick the Gray, a wizard who lived on the edge of Kuldahar and dedicated his life to the study of mythals.

## Ginger Lady

The Ginger Lady was a Shou trade caravel that sailed across the waters of the Eastern Heartlands during the mid–14 century DR.

Description
The Ginger Lady was about  long, and its figurehead was carved into the shape of a dragon's head.

Uses
The ship was used by Prince Kao Chou Tang and the Shou Ministry of Spices to transport gold, jewels, silk goods, spices, and ylang blossoms to the Ginger Palace for trade to locates across the rest of the Dragon Coast. Unbeknownst to Minister Hsieh Han Liu, Prince Tang also used the vessel to smuggle illicit plants and flowers that could be used to formulate poisons, for use by the Cult of the Dragon.

History
In the, the Ginger Lady was en route to Ilipur when it was attacked by the dracolich Hethcypressarvil. The undead black dragon wanted to kill Minister Hsieh aboard the vessel, so that he would not replace Prince Tang as the head of the Ginger Palace, and interfere with the Dragon Cult's poison trade in the region. Fortunately the ship was saved by the efforts of the Harpers agent Ruha, and the crew of the Storm Sprite.

## Giant ant lion

Giant ant lions were a monstrous variety of the insects of the same name.

Description

Descriptions of these creatures varied. Some said they resembled giant dragonflies, with larval forms that were the size of a warhorse and looked like fat-bodied louses. Others would say that they looked like a cross between a mole and a giant ant.

Their bodies were gray or sandy brown in color, completely covered by a leather exoskeleton with patches of coarse black bristles. These bristles were sensitive to both movement and odors. They had six, thick legs with sharp claws and flat bristles - they used these for digging and brushing away loose soil respectively. Their eyes were deep-set and beady.

The most prominent feature of these creatures were their enormous mandibles, extending  from the mouth and its rows of jagged teeth. These mandibles were silvery gray and razor-edged, with a single barb centered on the inner ridge that they used to impale and hold prey.

Abilities
The bites of these creatures had a paralytic poison.

Combat
Rather than stalking or hunting prey, these creatures dug deep tapering pits that measured  in diameter. They did this by digging backwards, hurling the loose soil out in circles. They then got within these pits and buried themselves under a covering of loose gravel, sand, soil, or stones. Then waited for prey to fall into their pit. Sometimes their mandibles protruded from this covering. They were skilled at knocking down any prey that tried to climb their way out of these pits, sometimes throwing loose soil at them.

Once a giant ant lion had prey grasped in its mandibles, they would not let go until either one of them was dead. They would impale prey with their mandible barbs, crush them with their mandibles, and grind their mandibles back and forth in a sawing motion.

History
In 1362 DR, giant ant lions could be found in various parts of the city of Kuo Meilan.

Ecology
Giant ant lions mated once per year during the mid-summer season. When females entered their mating cycle they released aromatic secretions, detectable only by the males of the species, and would continue to do so until a male entered their lair. Once a male entered their lair, the pair engaged in a mating ritual in which they clicked their mandibles at each other over a period of three full days. This clicking put them in a trance-like state, keeping them from eating and sleeping. Once the ritual ceased, the pair would mate and the male would leave the nest.

Females of the species typically laid one to four eggs in the week following mating, which they buried in the ground. These eggs hatched after six months, which newborns immediately burrowing away to construct lairs of their own. These creatures spent a long portion of their lives within a larval state.
Diet
Giant ant lions were a carnivorous species. They were liable to devour any creature that fell into their pits, though they had a preference for giant insects. Typically eating one or more giant insects per day, particularly giant ants.
Habitats
These giant insects were typically found in the hills, mountains, plains, badlands, or desert fringes of regions with temperate or tropical climates. Often in areas where other giant insects lived.

On the continent of Faerûn, giant ant lions could be found in the Marching Mountains of Calimshan.

In the Hordelands, these creatures could be found in the country of Ra-Khati. In the neighboring region of Kara-Tur, they could be found in tropical and subtropical deserts. Further south, they could be found in the badlands, hills, plains, and mountains of Zakhara. In the sea that enclosed that continent, the Crowded Sea, giant ant lions could be found on Nada al-Hazan. Across the sea they could be found in the plains of the continent of Maztica.

Lairs
The lair of a giant ant lion consisted of their pit trap and a narrow passage leading away from it. This led to a large chamber, where the giant ant lion would sleep and eat their prey. These chambers had another passage that lead to the surface, which they used as either an escape route or to drop the remnants of their prey outside.
Usages
The bodies of giant ant lions largely had no commercial value, though some primitive cultures repurposed their mandibles into plows.

## Giant wolf spider

A giant wolf spider, sometimes just referred to as a wolf spider, was a medium-sized beast that was smaller than a typical giant spider.

Description
Giant wolf spiders were smaller than usual giant spiders, but were more agile. As such, they were much faster at crawling and climbing. Like giant spiders, they had the ability to climb awkwardly shaped walls and hang upside down on ceilings, sense when a creature was caught in their web, and travel well across webbing.

They were less strong than giant spiders, but were more intelligent, robust, and wise. As such, when combined with their superior speed, they were fantastic stalkers. This allowed them to hunt their foes across plains, underground, or in burrows.

Wolf spiders were said to have very good eyesight and potentially even a basic sense of smell. In addition, they tracked their prey by the use of their scent-detecting organs, which were located in their pedipalps.

Female wolf spiders were incredibly protective of their offspring, which often numbered in the hundreds. As such, they were known to carry their offspring in little cocoons under their stomachs, or sometimes the offspring would even ride their mothers.

Combat

Due to their great tracking abilities, these arachnids often descended from rooftops or hid under spongy surfaces to surprise their enemies.

Giant wolf spiders relied on their bite in order to take down their foes. As well as piercing their opponents, their bite was poisonous. If the poison caused their foe to become unconscious, the poison would remain in their system for an hour, as well as causing them to be paralyzed.

Ecology
Giant wolf spiders made their homes in many different habitats, such as deserts, grassland, forests, and hills. Specifically, wolf spiders often made their homes in silk-lined burrows.

These giant arachnids were known to lurk in many places across Faerûn, such as Omu in Chult, the Western Heartlands, and the Braeryn of the Underdark city of Menzoberranzan. Giant wolf spiders were known to skulk around some areas in Maztica, such as in the hilltop ruins of the Ixtul Valley.

A great threat to a female wolf spider was another female wolf spider. On sight, they would fight each other to the death, and the victor would bear responsibility to raise the fallen spider's young.

History
In Eleint 1367 DR, an adventurer slew a huge yellow giant wolf spider in the woods south of Shadowdale. Somehow, it emitted a magical glow, even after it had died. Not willing to give up such a grand trophy for nothing, the adventurer took it to Jhaele Silvermane of the Old Skull Inn. She traded the glowing spider corpse for a week's stay at the inn with as much ale as the seller could manage.

In the 14 century DR, the evil goddess Loviatar was known to send wolf spiders to her followers in order to show her favor. In the 15 century DR, many druids of the Realms had the ability to wild shape into giant wolf spiders.

Appendix
Trivia
 The Sword Coast Legends manual outlines how one can create one's own characters. The example given is a pink wolf spider called Charlotte who asks adventurers to find her eggs.

## Giant Zakharan rat

The giant Zakharan rat was a large rodent that was known to dwell in Zakhara.

Description
Giant Zakharan rats were cousins of the giant rat but were much larger. Some specimens grew to up to  in length. This was not including their long tail. They were covered in a brownish fur that was filthy and carried communicable diseases.

Combat
Giant Zakharan rats were vicious fighters, attacking with their sharp teeth and claws until either their target was killed or the rat itself. Since they lived in packs, a large creature such as a humanoid could be taken down by multiple giant Zakharan rats.

Ecology
These large rats were often found in subterranean locations such as dungeons. Their skeletal structure had the ability to be compressed, allowing the giant Zakharan rat to squeeze through cracks and other tight openings. Giant Zakharan rats lived in groups of no larger than a dozen individuals. They ate basically anything they could, both living and dead.

## Giant's bane

Giant's bane were enchanted sling bullets created for defense against giants.

Powers
Giant's bane bullets were enchanted to deal additional damage when used against giants.

History
Giant's bane ammunition was created by halflings, defending their homes from giant raids. Over the years, these bullets were the result of many giants' demise. By late 14 century DR, giant's bane bullets were quite often encountered across the Sword Coast as well as the Moonsea regions of Faerûn.

## Giant flying spider

Giant flying spiders, also known as chasm spiders or hunting spiders, were a rare species of subterranean spider related to the much smaller flying spider.

Description
The most distinguishing feature of this species was that they had translucent, cobweb-like wings. They used these to aid and steer great leaps, traveling up to  horizontally on average. So long as their wings were intact and had room to beat, they could fall almost any distance without being harmed.

The body of a hunting spider measured around  in diameter.

Personality
These spiders were of average intellect for a creature of their nature. This made them capable of being trained and of recognizing people by their gestures, smell, and voice. They generally lacked the intellectual capability to recognize if someone was a spellcaster. Hunting spiders also disliked bright lights and had a fear of fire, meaning that a campfire was typically sufficient to deter them.

Abilities
These creatures had a deadly venomous bite, which took effect ten to thirty minutes after their victim was bitten. Giant flying spiders also had the trait of truevision.

Combat
Much like a bird, giant flying spiders would use their airborne capabilities to ambush potential prey.

History
In 1492 DR, giant flying spiders were in use by members of House Auvryndar of Menzoberranzan as they worked to establish a foothold in Undermountain.

Society
Giant flying spiders were generally not known to sleep.

Diet
These spiders were an omnivorous species. Some of their favorite prey included small animals, goblins, kobolds, and even other lesser species of spiders. They generally did not try to prey upon humans unless they were alone, unarmored, and had no significant source of flame near them.

Homelands
Giant flying spiders exclusively inhabited subterranean areas. In Faerûn, they could particularly be found living in underground caverns beneath the Goblin Marches, Stonelands, and Cormanthor. They occasionally came up to the surface to hunt, particularly at night.

Relationships
Some individuals were known to train giant flying spiders to act as guardian animals, which would remain loyal so long as they were regularly fed. Many were known to serve wizards as familiars, especially drow wizards.

## Gilern Ghostwise

Gilern Ghostwise was a ghostwise halfling that lived with his kin in the wilds of the Sword Coast North in the late 15 century DR.

Activities
Gilern and his band lived in the wilderness near Raven Cliff Beach, not far from Cragmire Keep. They lived secretive lives, conducting raids in order to secure the resources they needed to live.

History
In the late 1370s DR, Gilern's band had only recently moved into the area. A passing merchant sighted them and mistook them for "feral midgets". Learning of this, in the, the halfling Cecil Proudfoot hired a fellow halfling adventurer find them so the ghostwise and halfling travelers could help each other against local marauders.

However, a member of Gilern's band was struck with an arrow shot by a gnoll blood-tracker aligned with the Blackdagger Bandits and they sheltered in the bushes. Gilern requested aid from the adventurer in fighting the gnolls in order for his kin to recover and his clan to get to safety. After that, they promised to harass the bandits from the forest.

## Gillian Cantilar

Gillian Cantilar was the half-elven ranger who dwelt on the top of the hill that later became the hamlet of Gillian's Hill and was the town's namesake.

Description
Gillian Cantilar was said to be a creature of great beauty.

History
Long before 14 century DR, Gillian lived in a lone house atop the wooded mound at the northern edge of the area that eventually became known as Gillian's Hill. The ranger was considered a hero and defender by merchants who traveled along the Trade Way. Gillian was one of the very few who set out to spy on local bandits, poachers, and make sure caravans remained unspoiled by the many dangers of the road.

Eventually, the area of the ranger's razed home was taken by the elevated fire cairn, used to warn Daggerford of encroaching enemy armies of Dragonspear Castle and Serpent Hills. However, the makeshift tower was often used as a flophouse for various unsavory elements. In 1372 DR, a group known as the Gillian's Hill Gang was occupying the spot while targeting passing traders and merchants.

## Gillian's bow

Gillian's bow was the enchanted longbow that belonged to Gillian Cantilar, the defender of the Trade Way and the namesake of the hamlet of Gillian's Hill.

Powers
Gillian's bow possessed a +2 enchantment. The weapon was also fitted with an ingenious sighting mechanism of gnomish creation. It gave Gillian's bow range, dramatically longer than other longbows, and gave the weapon the ability to deal devastating critical damage when its arrows reached their targets dead-center.

History
This enchanted bow faithfully served Gillian Cantilar, the half-elven ranger hero of the Trade Way. By 1372 DR, Gillian's bow was in the hands of Gillian's Hill Gang, a group of bandits who took over the fire cairn tower on the hill's summit and menaced the locals and traveling merchants.

Gillian's Hill Gang was a makeshift group of bandits assembled in the ruined tower of Gillian Cantilar on the summit of Gillian's Hill, turning it into a flophouse. They started robbing travelers and caravans that traveled along the Trade Way. Eventually, they adopted the name of Gillian's Hill Gang and became a big nuisance for Sejellus Krimp, the owner of a distribution company that operated out of the hamlet. He posted a bounty on the gang in the city of Daggerford and eventually, the individual known as the Hero of Daggerford claimed the 500 gold coins after disbanding and possibly killing the gang members.

## Giant wasp poison

Giant wasp poison was a type of venom secreted by the giant variation of regular wasps, most commonly in Faerûn - found inhabiting the forest of Cormanthor.

Effects
Giant wasp venom was often used to coat weapons or injuring traps. When introduced into the target's bloodstream, the poison stiffened the affected creature's body, dramatically reducing their nimbleness and dexterity, eventually, leading to the victim's possible death.

Giant wasp poison was also could be used by assassins to poison foodstuffs.

Usages
Giant wasp venom was used for the final step of wands of enemy detection creation. The wands were polished using this insect substance.

History
In 1369 DR, traps of the Crypt of Kesefehon, coated in giant wasp venom, were still potent and deadly, name centuries after being set, predating the coming of the Weeping War and fall of Myth Drannor.

Notable Users
Organizations
Drow slavers were known to be armed with several dozes of giant wasp poison.
Fire Knives, the assassin guid from Cormyr, later from Westgate, who used giant wasp venom to coat their deadly blades and poison their targets' meals.
Individuals
Eshenesra, an elven rogue, assassin, and the sister of Thatoryl, who harbored bloodthirsty hate of humans circa 1371 DR.
Kaedthis, the chieftain of a tribe of lycanthropes from the forest of Cormanthor circa 1374 DR. She carried giant wasp poison and black lotus extract, ready for weapon application.

## Gimlet's portals

These were two unnamed portals, constructed by the Lantanese rock gnome pirate Gimlet Watersprecht some time in the 1370s DR with aid of a small crew, that formed a pirate trade route between the Inner Sea and Trackless Sea. Both portals were constructed to not allow entry by large ships and the key to activating them was casting any spell in the Illusion school of magic.

Location
One of these portals was located in the Trackless Sea, about a day's journey east of Lantan. There it was situated between two  tall immovable rods that were  apart. These immovable rods were of a blue metal, identical in coloration to their surrounding water, making them barely visible to even those who knew about them. The activation buttons of these immovable rods were capped with metal hoods and fused into place, ensuring that they would not be deactivated.

The other portal was located within the a low yet wide, half-submerged cave of a small, uninhabited isle in the Pirate Isles of the Inner Sea. The cave was just large enough for a rowboat or narrower keelboat to fit through, though creatures taller than the average halfling or gnome had to duck, and  behind the portal was the end of the cave.

History
Following his construction of these portals, Gimlet began a quite lucrative operation of trading firearms to the Pirate Isles and raking in treasures from the nations that stood along the shores of the Inner Sea. The former activity notably caused a strong decrease in business for the pirates of the Nelanther Isles.

Notable Inhabitants
The portal on the Lantan side was guarded by a tojanida, who Gimlet had recruited to be part of his "crew".

## Girdle of lions

A girdle of lions was an enchanted belt that granted the wearer several powers related to felines.

Powers
When worn a girdle of lions allowed the wearer to speak with felines and made the felines in question regard the wearer in a very favorable light. The felines would often obey any reasonable commands given by the wearer. The wearer was also able to land from falls in such a manner that mitigated some of the harm from the landing. The girdle also made it easier for the wearer to move around in total silence. Lastly, the wearer could see in the dark just as if they were a feline.

## Giantspire Gap

The Giantspire Gap, also called the Giant Gap, was the main pass through the Giantspire Mountains separating Damara and Narfell.

Description
While it was the main pass through the mountains, it was more appropriately described as a region of foothills between the taller spurs of the Giantspire Mountains. The Long Road passed through the Gap between Damara to the west and Narfell to the east, though this route was seldom used, owing to marauding monsters and hobgoblins. During the winter, the Gap was completely blocked by snow.

Trade
Although treacherous, the Gap was the primary trade route connecting Damaran merchants to the Nar tribes.

History
In the mid-to-late 14 century DR, the Gap was claimed by both Damara and Narfell, as well as by Kardobbik, the hobgoblin king of the Giantspires.

As of the late 15 century DR, travelers through the Gap were subject both to headhunting raids by the bugbears of the Blood Mountain tribe as well as to paying a toll charged by the goblins of the Razor Heart tribe. The Razor Heart queen, Maaqua, was beleived to have a relationship with Damara that emboldened her "taxation" of the route.

Description
The young woman had sun-tanned skin, straggly brown hair and blue eyes.

Abilities
She was very agile and adept at climbing trees without giving notice of her movements.

History
Gillian was killed during her company's ill-fated journey through Spiderhaunt Wood during the Time of Troubles in the . She was the first of her fellows to die, her body impaled by the made bladed legs of a giant spider.

## Giantspine

Giantspine, or Jotunspine, was the largest island within the kingdom of Norheim in the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
Giantspine was centrally located among the Norheim Isles. It sat between the Trackless Sea and the Sea of Moonshae on Northwind Strait in the northwest corner of the Moonshae Isles. The Giantspine Range dominated much of the island and shielded the eastern coast from the storms that rolled in off of the Trackless Sea. Giantspine was unique among the rocky islands of Norheim in that it contained several pockets of forested land. It was the only one of the Norheim Isles that could meaningfully support agriculture or where mining was a contributor to the economy.

## Giantspine Range

The Giantspine Range was a mountain range found on the island of Giantspine in the Norheim Isles in the Moonshae Isles.

Geography
The mountain was a dominant feature of the island, stretching from end to end for over . Aside from the shoreline, and a small forested region in the northwest, the Giantspine Range covered the entire island. The village of Seawolf was found at the mountain's northern edge, and the capital of Hammerstaad was found at its southern edge.

History
The mountains were once home to Ualintharghar, a red dragon known in Ffolk legends as "the Devouring Flame". He was long-dead as of the mid-to-late 14 century DR.

Gildengloop was an insignificant settlement apart from one detail; it was one of the thirteen destinations of the teleport without error spell ward that protected the hidden lair of a wizard who resided in Nonthal circa the . The wizard's lair - Nonthal's Hold, was protected by thirteen gates that, in not deactivated with the correct command word, spirited the intruder away. Gildengloop was the destination of the ninth gate.

## Gilded armor

Gilded armor were elaborately decorated enchanted breastplates, worn by loyal ranking servants or the council of Samarach.

Powers
Gilded armor possessed +1 enchantment and enhanced the wearers' ability to assert dominance and intimidate creatures.

History
By the late 14 century DR, gilded armor was commonly worn by Captains of the Patrol in the nation of Samarach. In 1374 DR, one of these Captain, along with a band of patrolmen, invaded the hamlet of Nimbre, after hearing of the possible yuan-ti corruption. A group of adventurers hired by a merchant Sa'Sani walked in on the harassment of peaceful villagers. Even though the outcome of the encounter remained unclear, some said that the gilded armor might've changed hands that day.

## Gift of life

Gift of life was an Elven High Magic wizard spell that restored undead to life.

Effects
Unwilling undead targets could resist the spell's effects but found it was very difficult to do so. A target would be restored to life just how it was right before it died. If the target died of natural causes, then the spell restored it to life as it was ten years before its death. The restoration was shocking for the target, leaving them stunned for nearly an hour as they slowly realized what just occurred.

Though the restored target would still be wearing the same clothing and carrying the same gear that they were before the spell was cast, the abilities and skills they possessed were appropriate for how they were in life before their death. The new chance at life sometimes prompted wicked undead, such as vampires or liches, to repent from their old way to lead good lives.

Components
In addition to verbal and somatic components, this spell required as a material component a gold medallion shaped like the sun to cast.

## Girdle of Stromnos

The girdle of Stromnos was an enchanted belt created by Presdon of the Vast.

Description
The belt was a plain in appearance but distinctly wide girdle.

Powers
The girdle of Stromnos possessed a simple enchantment that significantly enhanced its wearer's strength, making them as strong as a hill giant.

History
The girdle of Stromnos was created by Presdon of the Vast several centuries before 1281 DR. The enchanter once encountered a hungry hill giant named Stromnos while gathering spell components. Presdon survived the encounter and named the belt after Stromnos. The girdle then was passed along to the enchanter's bodyguard Dendes the Wry. The belt was lost some time late outside Ordulin as Dendes was killed in the line of duty.

For the several centuries, the girdle of Stromnos was thought lost, until it reappeared in the . The belt was worn by Flozem, one of the two bodyguards employed by Marketh of the Dorn's Deep in the Spine of the World.

## Gizekhtet

Gizekhtet was one of two divine domains belonging to the Pharaonic and Mulhorandi goddess of love, Isis.

Cosmology

In the Great Wheel cosmology, Gizekhtet was one of the three triangular divine realms that made up Heliopolis. It often overlapped with Memphiria, the divine realm of Osiris, due to him being Isis's spouse.

Under the World Tree cosmology, it was likewise part of Heliopolis, which in that cosmology was a neutral plane that was shared by all members of the Mulhorandi pantheon.
Planar Traits
In the Great Wheel cosmology, it was said that there existed no restrictions upon the spells that one could cast while in Gizekhtet. However, if one were to use magic for evil purposes they would be stripped of their spellcasting ability by Isis, as she considered it a betrayal of her good will, and would only regain them upon leaving Heliopolis.

Geography
In both cosmologies Gizekhtet was a fertile desert realm. In the Great Wheel, it was said to have a sloping landscape and sported a moderate temperature. The majority of fauna in Heliopolis, as well as most flora were, found in Gizekhtet. Its sloping landscape was covered in thick grass and dotted with fig and palm trees.

Notable Locations
 Gizekhtet (city), a city inhabited by the petitioners of this realm. In the World Tree cosmology, the area around this city was the most lush in all of Heliopolis.
 The River Isis, a waterway that ran through either the realm or its city, depending upon the cosmology. In the Great Wheel cosmology, it was said to solely run through the city, while in the World Tree cosmology it was said to run through both the city and much of Heliopolis, blessing both its fields and inhabitants.

## Gizekhtet (city)

Gizekhtet, also known as Quietude, was a city in the divine realm of the same name.

Description
The buildings of Gizekhtet were constructed primarily from brick and white stucco. The River Isis ran through its center, providing cool clear water to its inhabitants. Within the city limits, this water acted as holy water.

Government
Gizekhtet had a matriarchal government, though all were judged equally under its law and the sexes lived without enmity. The goddess Isis lived in and watched over the city. Under her purview, the city was ruled by Lamia Nightblossom, a tiefling wizardess whose character was marked by compassion, fairness, and justice. Isis trusted her implicitly with rulership of Gizekhtet whenever she was away.

Inhabitants
This city was primarily inhabited by the petitioners of Isis, many of which came from the nation of Mulhorand, whom lived lives of balance and moderation in all matters. The people lived in true equality with one another, never lacking or having an overabundance of anything essential. Though there were still those who could comparatively be considered somewhat "poor" or "wealthy," as well as craftsfolk and workers.

## Giantsize

Giantsize was a divine magic spell granted to priests of Arvoreen, the Wary Sword. It temporarily increased the size and strength of one halfling to that of a hill giant, along with everything they were wearing or carrying.

Effects
This spell only worked on halflings, and could be cast on oneself or another halfling that could be touched. The target and all its clothing and carried items grew proportionately until the target was the size of a hill giant, so a short sword became a bastard sword, for example. The spell granted the halfling the strength of a hill giant, but not the toughness of their hide. Giantsize lasted for at least nine minutes (longer for more experienced casters) and then the target and all its enlarged accoutrements shrank back to normal. If cast on oneself, the caster could cancel the spell at any time. To all observers, the target of giantsize appeared to be a hill giant and the change could not be detected by any means except true seeing.

Components
This spell required only verbal, and somatic components to cast, including touching the target halfling.

## Gilded Gallows

The Gilded Gallows was a ruthless thieves' guild operating in the Western Heartlands and the Sword Coast as of the late 15 century DR.

Base of Operations
The guild was formed and based in Elturgard.

History
As of the late 15 century DR, the Gilded Gallows was prospering and its influence was extending along the Sword Coast, with operatives as far north as Neverwinter. Its symbol—a gold noose—had become instantly recognizable among criminals in the region.

By the, rumors had surfaced that a high-ranking member had betrayed the guild to steal a large sum of money, and so members of the guild were motivated to track down this traitor and the wealth he had secreted away. In fact, the traitor was Tarak, who had been ordered to kill his own lover and had instead chosen to flee the guild. His lover was ultimately assassinated by another member of the guild, and the story was twisted to accuse Tarak of murder and theft.

Members
Guildmembers were known as "Gilders", and included not only thieves but assassins, poisoners, gamblers, smugglers, and goblins. They recruited even very young children into their ranks, and trained them in stealth and deception. Some high-ranking members had tattoos featuring the guild's golden noose symbol.

Notable Members
 Tarak, who fled the guild and his life of crime to live on Stormwreck Isle.

## Girdle of fire

A girdle of fire was an enchanted girdle providing protection from fire.

Description
A girdle of fire typically covered the user's midriff, was made of hardened leather, and was fastened with lace at the front; the leather there was sewn in layers that resembled flames, with the points of the flames pointing at the wearer's sternum.

Powers
The girdle itself made its wearer immune to nonmagical fire; it was always immune to fire itself. It also granted them immunity to their own fire magic, and considerably eased the avoidance of other fire magic. Even flames that were not completely avoided were powerfully mitigated.

It also allowed the use of flaming sphere, fireball, delayed blast fireball, and fly once a day each. The last spell left a visible trail of flame, albeit it wouldn't ignite anything.

History
Talatha Vaerovree found such a girdle in a tomb within Orsraun Mountains, in a tomb where the bones of a renegade Netherese sorceress, Nellaura Orbrynsar, had somehow found themselves.

The targeted animal was capable of understanding spoken words and simple concepts. However, the spell did not actually increase the intelligence of the targeted animal.

## Ginger

Ginger, or darsargar in dried and powdered form, was a fragrant and strong-tasting root of a plant of the same name, related to turmeric, used for its aroma, taste, and medicinal properties.

Availability
 Shou Lung was a major exporter of expensive ginger. In the 15 century DR, Shou ginger was exported along with Shou migrants to the Xiousing district of Marsember, where it was grown in Shen's Herb Garden.
 The Free Cities of Parsanic were an exporter of ginger. In the late 14 century DR, Rishi Saubhari was a bahrana ginger-prince of the Free Cities. In the wider Utter East, the streets of the nation of Doegan often wafted of ginger, cinnamon, and other aromas, both pleasant and foul.
 Ginger was exported out of Port Nyanzaru in Chult into Baldur's Gate in the late 15 century DR by Tendai and Khennen Shore.
 The Thayan enclave of Proskur in the Eastern Heartlands sold spices including ginger.
 The famous Aurora's Whole Realms Catalogue sold ginger in  portions for wizardly use for the price of a single silver piece. Honeyed ginger preserves were sold for a whopping price of 50 gp, and fresh ginger in colombine or string form was available for 10 and 6 gp, respectively. Ginger marmalade was available for 20 gp for those with a sweet tooth. And finally, gingerbread made with Shou ginger was on offer, sold for a single gold piece per tin.

Usages
Cuisine
 Waterdhavians indulged in expensive Shou ginger dipped in just as expensive Maztican chocolate, accompanied by warm sugared milk.
 Moon Mountain Brewery of the Savage Frontier created a popular mulled brew using ground ginger among other ingredients.
 In the capital city of Cormyr, Suzail, powdered ginger was used as an ingredient in preparation of the yeti's breath alcoholic beverage served at the Hidden Lady inn.
 Sliced oranges preserved in ginger were consumed in Darromar in Tethyr. Baskets of preserved fruit as such were common if not cheap gestures of goodwill. In Mosstone, ginger and carrot soup was a popular staple of the Oak-Father's Boon inn.
 In similar fashion, The Old Wheel eatery in the Amnian city of Crimmor served venison drizzled with honey-ginger.
 In Aglarondan towns like Veltalar, ginger cakes were a part of feasts hosted by those in the position of power.
 Gurneth's Goblet in Ravens Bluff served shrimp cooked in ginger paste.
 The root was used in baking a treacle-sweetened Faerûnean treat—ginger biscuits.
 In Calimshan's "asarth" cooking method of sautéing in spiced oil or butter, ginger was among the flavoring agents used.
 Ginger beer was a fermented drink made with ginger by dwarves of Eartheart in the East Rift, and a common drink in Lapaliiya, the Tashalar, and the southern Vilhon Reach.
 Folk of Halruaa appreciated a good cup of green tea with honey and ginger, sometimes spiked with strong Haerlu brandy.
 In Calimshan and Tashalar, where drinking of black coffee was common, some would add ginger to the beverage to alter its flavor.
 In Turmish grounded ginger was a commonly available spice due to imports and trade.
 On the Malatran Plateau in Kara-Tur, ginger was used as a pickling spice, together with mustard seeds, black peppercorns, red chiles, cloves, allspice, coriander, and mace.

Miscellaneous
 In Iriaebor, a city in the Western Heartlands, inns offered their guests hot wet towels scented with ginger.
 Some believed that ginger had minor medicinal properties to help with cramps and the common cold, as well as act as a stimulant.
 A type of an undead creature mummy possessed a strong aroma of various herbs and chemicals used in its creation; among them was ginger.
 The spell spice could produce several types of spices and herbs, including ginger.

## Girdaur

The Girduar family was a clan of disgraced dwarven artisans and master craftsfolk as of the late 15 century DR.

History
By the time of their downfall in the 15 century DR, the family had originally come into prominence within the past two dwarven generations. They were ruined both financially and reputationally when their enemies stole their masterworks and tools. The young Naomlen Girdaur devoted the next decades of her life training as a rogue and assassin, and sought to reclaim the family's lost works and lost honor. She worked with groups as varied as the Zhentarim and the Open Lord of Waterdeep to accomplish this singular goal.

By the late 15 century DR, Naomlen had reclaimed enough pieces of her family's masterworks to use them to forge a pair of enchanted kukris known as the kukris of cunning, which she wielded in continued pursuit of her goal.

Members
 Naomlen Girdaur, a rogue and Zhentarim assassin who sought to reclaim the family's lost works.
 Naomlen's great-grandmother, who was said to have crafted the elusive ring prior to the family's initial fame.

## Giandro Holfast

Giandro Holfast was a master shipwright of Targos in Icewind Dale in the late 15 century DR. In the 1480s DR, he was the Speaker of Targos and its representative to the council of the Ten Towns and also one of the leaders of the Dockworker's Guild.

Description
At middle age, Giandro had dark hair and well-callused hands from a life of work. He had a strong jaw and could be considered handsome but for his constantly frowning. His voice was stern and gravelly.

Personality
With pride in his work and in his town, Giandro was resolute that Targos should be self-reliant.

Activities
As well as being a master shipwright working at the docks, as Speaker, Giandro led the town and represented it on the Ten Towns council. He was bothered by Easthaven's rise to prominence and resentful of Bryn Shander's existing prominence and what he saw as its manipulations to maintain that position and keep the other towns in its shadow. While not hostile to any of the other Speakers, he rejected any proposal that he thought would make Targos subservient to another of the Ten Towns.

In addition, he stood among the Dockworker's Guild's leaders. He led an effort to extend the Dockworker's Guild's influence outside of Targos, to the other nine towns. While Targos might lose some of its competitive edge, Giandro reasoned it would gain greater influence and make Targos the equal of Bryn Shander.

History
He was a Targosan born and bred.

During his leadership in the, Targos, and the Ten Towns as a whole, were faced with an attempt by Luskan's Arcane Brotherhood to take political power via Vaelish Gant, an influx of items crafted out of black ice that corrupted their owners, and the increasing powers of the cultists of Auril.

## Giran

Giran was a commoner child of Hannah from the City of Coin, Athkatla, the capital of Amn in the late 14 century DR.

History
in the, Giran and his mother visited a circus performing at the center of Athkatla's Center District, in Waukeen's Promenade. Unfortunately, the circus' tent fell victim to a mad gnome illusionist Kalah, who transformed the tent's interior into a fanciful tower filled with guests and performers, ensorcelled to appear as monsters. Giran's mother was trapped inside, transformed into a giant spider. The child was left outside of the spellbound tent, crying for his missing mother. When the illusion was broken and the gnome defeated, Giran was reunited with Hannah.

## Ginni Lannon

Ginni Lannon was a human inhabitant of the village of West Harbor on the Sword Coast North in the 1370s DR.

Description
Ginni had blonde hair affixed in a tight ponytail. She had a face that showed determination and her bright eyes were clear and focused.

Relationships
Ginni Lannon was Pitney Lannon's wife and a fellow soldier. They had four children – Pitney Junior, Kiri, Kyl, and Kaye Lannon.

History
In the, soon after, the Mere of Dead Men was briefly consumed by a short but deadly conflict known as the Second War of the Shadow. As the village was being rebuild and recovered, the town's mage, Tarmas, disappeared. Ginni and Pitney Lannon volunteered to follow the wizard's tracks and attempt the rescue. The Lannons discovered Tarmas captured by lizardfolk within the Dragon Caves south of West Harbor. They quickly returned the man back to the village. However, unbeknownst to them, Tarmas they retrieved was a magically disguised black dragon. Then the deception was revealed, Pitney joined the soldiers in defending the village against Voaraghamanthar and Waervaerendor, who wanted to take over the village and its Harvest Mead brewery. It was up to adventurers to aid the soldiers or the dragons.

## Giant starfish

Giant starfish or giant sunstar were monstrously sized cousins of the mundane undersea creatures known as starfish.

Description
These creatures were largely similar in appearance to mundane starfish, but had ten or twelve rays that radiated from their centers, rather than five. Their bodies could measure anywhere from  across, though it was relatively rare to encounter any larger than  across and those that reached above those lengths were thought of as mostly rumors.

The underside of their central body and its rays were covered in sticky suckers and if a ray was severed it could regenerate back within a period of one to four months. Their bodies ranged in color from yellow, orange, red, pink, and tan. The hue of this coloration ranged from pale pastels to being quite vibrant.

Behavior
The intelligence of these creatures was above that of a mundane starfish, ranging from being semi-intelligent to having a low degree of intelligence.

Combat
When attacking sailing vessels a giant starfish would approach and maneuver itself beneath their underside. It would then either attempt to pull as many people overboard, then retreat, or grapple the ship from below for a more involved attack. The latter sometimes broke masts, caused holes in the deck or hull of sailing vessels, and could potentially knock crew overboard with their shaking. Once its prey was grappled, a giant starfish would try to pry open and tear at the hull with its rays in order to search for any creatures inside.

When actively grappling a sailing vessel, a giant starfish would not retreat from their captive ship until at least half of its rays were either disabled or severed. When they finally engaged in this retreat, the starfish would try to carry away a victim in each of its still-functioning rays.

History
Around 1492 DR, the parts of the Caverns of Ooze that were altered by Halaster to accomodate his marid prisoner Ichthyglug were inhabited by a number of benign giant starfish.

Ecology
These creatures lived solitary lives, actively wandering in search of prey, only interacting with others of their kind during mating season. Those with ten rays never mated with those that had twelve or vice versa and were likely to fight each other.
Diet
Giant starfish were carnivorous creatures. They preyed upon a wide variety of giant undersea life that were limited in their mobility, such as giant clams. On rare occasions they would try to tussle with whales, giant octopi, and even krakens. They often preferred preying upon sailing vessels, even if it meant trailing after them for many miles.
Habitats
Giant starfish were sighted in large saltwater oceans all across the worlds of Toril, Oerth, and Krynn. Even the inland ocean of interior Faerûn, the Sea of Fallen Stars, in its Lower Depths zone.The "Lower Depths" zone of the Inner Sea referred to depths of roughly  beneath the ocean. Beyond the Prime Material, giant starfish could be found in parts of the Domains of Dread.

Large caves and underwater trenches were the typical lairs of giant starfish, with crevices and niches being used to store any extra food or treasure they had. These lairs were rarely approached by other undersea life.

Rumors & Legends
On many worlds, it was rumored that a freshwater sub-species could be found in large inland lakes.

Girdle of ogre blood was a unique belt imbued with protective magics and created by Caldinar the Wily some years before the late 13 century DR.

Description
Caldinar's girdle of ogre blood was a broad-sided belt made of tanned ogre hide and decorated with lozenges filled with black ogre blood.

Powers
This item possessed regenerative enchantments that made the wearer slowly yet continually heal. Additionally, the wearer of the belt could summon five ogres per day that would remain under the summoner's command and disappear seventeen minutes later.

Caldinar the Wily created the girdle of ogre blood some years before the, along with many other magic items to protect himself during adventuring. Caldinar often wore the best and parted ways with it after death when his companions auctioned the girdle off. Eventually, the belt found its way to Icewind Dale by 1281 DR.

## Gigante

The gigantes, known by some simply as giants, were a race of monstrous elder giants created by the primeval goddess Gaea.On page 105 of Legends & Lore the gigantes are referred to as simply "the Giants". This is carried over to page 2 of the Living City module No Time to Lose, which copies much of the former text verbatim in order to provide backstory for the greater titans in the adventure. Despite their other name, gigantes were nothing like the true giants found on the Prime Material.

Description
A gigante was large in stature and had a rather human-like appearance, but they had serpents in place of where feet should be or serpent tails where legs should be. They could move with incredible speed.

Abilities
The gigantes possessed incredible power, being living embodiments of volcanic power. Each individual gigantes had some sort of unique power, such as a regenerative nature or a fear aura.

Society
Homeland
These creatures could be found on Othrys, the first layer of the plane of Carceri.
Relationships
Gigantes harbored a deep seething hatred for the deities that made up the Olympian pantheon. They would never pass up an opportunity to cause some form of harm to either them or their minions.

History
The goddess Gaea convinced one of her children, the greater titan Cronus, to overthrow his father Uranus and thus avenge her mistreatment, in retaliation for him having locked away their more monstrous children. Cronus did so, wounding his father so severely that he allegedly perished. The blood from his wounds fell upon Gaea, fertilizing her once more, causing the creation of the Furies and the gigantes. However, Cronus went back on his word and refused to free his monstrous brethren. Furious, Gaea laid a curse upon Cronus, proclaiming that one day his own children would usurp him in turn. Eventually Gaea's curse came to pass and Cronus was usurped by his own son Zeus, who alongside his siblings led the way for the creation of the Olympian pantheon and the imprisonment of their forefathers, the greater titans, in the plane of Carceri.

Not long after the gigantes challenged the rule of the newly formed Olympian pantheon, having been directed against them by their mother Gaea in an effort to free the greater titans. However, the Olympians held fast against the gigantes and ultimately overcame them. The gigantes were then imprisoned in Carceri alongside the titans.

Millenia later, in the,Despite that Seven Millennia of Realms Fiction states that this novel occurs in 1369 DR, the novel is actually set later than this. Page 359 states that the events in the novel are "3 years" after the events of Prince of Lies (itself set in 1368 DR). Furthermore, in the first couple of chapters, Malik el Sami states to have lurked near Candlekeep for "years" after the events Prince of Lies (this must be true because the caliph received a letter detailing the events of Prince of Lies prior to sending Malik on his mission). Specifically, the following is written in a letter: "Oghma gave the tome to a mortal and bade her travel forth and hide, blessing her with a diamond amulet that would conceal her from all the gods of Faerûn", which happened in 1368 DR (in the previous novel). This is further supported by Malik claiming that he "had returned from years of spying" and even his superiors stating that "el Sami has, of necessity, been blind to the progress of the One Church over the last few years". a gigante appeared on Toril and attacked the settlement of Elventree in Cormanthor. The goddess Mystra sent one of her avatars to the scene to do battle with gigante and protect the settlement.

## Gisloi

Gisloi was a tasloi cleric of Loitasloitas and one of the two lieutenants of the Flotilla of DEATH.

Description
Like other tasloi, Gisloi had bright green skin, black eyes, and black hair. He stood  tall, weighed around, and had a somewhat pudgy build that he hid behind his clothes. Whenever he tried to speak Common it came out in a high pitch.

Personality
Gisloi was smarter than most of his tasloi kin, able to speak quite eloquently in their native tongue. He was also somewhat of a religious tyrant, teaching that it was heretical to disobey priests of their native faith.

Abilities
Gisloi was capable of casting spells from the spheres of All, Animal, Combat, Healing, and Protection. He particularly knew the spells bless, cure light wounds, and speak with animals.

Possessions
Gisloi typically wore a flashy set of red and yellow robes. Like the other tasloi on the Flotilla of DEATH, he was typically armed with a javelin. Notable among his possessions was a whistle of shrieking, made of ivory, that he wore around his neck. He would blow this whistle whenever their fleet entered a military engagement, stirring on the hearts of his fellow crewmen.

History
Gisloi was born sometime around the, on the island of Tasloiomitasloi.Port of Ravens Bluff (pp. 42, 45) states that Charles O'Kane became Mayor 20 years ago, which would set it in 1361 DR. Working backwards from this, Gisloi's birth year can be extrapolated. Sometime later Spitvah McGurk discovered the island and struck up a bargain with the native tasloi to form a small seafaring fleet called the Flotilla of DEATH, wherein Gisloi would be promoted to the role of lieutenant alongside Farloi. Out of the two, he uniquely acted as a sort of spiritual and moral leader for the tasloi that made up the fleet.

## Girdle of Brimir's strength

The girdle of Brimir's strength was a unique variant of girdle of giant strength that was made by Brimir, a renowned frost giant brewer in Jotunheim, as a gift to a group of adventurers from the city of Ravens Bluff.

Description
This girdle was made from a strip of rawhide that Brimir had cut off his own giant-sized belt and had a crimson hue from being thoroughly stained with his blood. The girdle's clasp was carved from the tooth of a bulette.  All along the girdle's length, Giantish runes were carved into it.

Powers
Enchanted with rune magic, the girdle of Brimir's strength gave its wearer tremendous strength, much like a standard girdle of giant strength. Where the belt differed in effect was causing the wearer to grow to a height of . These effects only lasted for up to four hours per day. After the effect was deactivated the wearer temporarily felt weaker than before and needed to rest for around twice the amount of time that the effect was active for.

History
Sometime around the 14 century DR, a group of adventurers from Ravens Bluff journeyed to Jotunheim in search of the legendary Well of Mimir so they could learn how to destroy a powerful githyanki artifact known only as the Great Machine.

According to some sources they came to Brimir's hall as honored guests of his friend Habrok Icecaster, having proven their worth to him and his friends by hunting a bulette. The giants gathered in the hall felt indifferent towards the guests, so to break tensions Brimir wagered a contest of four tests for the adventurers - eating, drinking, running, and wrestling. He was even willing to wager three magic items if they would wager three comparably permanent or major charged items.

In eating they faced off against the fire giant Logi and had to reach the middle of a  trencher of stew. In drinking they faced no one, but had to drink alcohol from a massive horn as fast as they could. In running they faced the young giant Hugi and had to make it to the far end of the hall. Finally, in wrestling they faced Brimir's old mother Elli, as the giants felt any other competitor would make things unfair.

Some claimed the adventurers managed to beat all four tests and for showing such prowess Brimir chose to give them an additional gift, creating the girdle of Brimir's strength.

Giddeon the Believer was a mage philosopher and the creator of the enchanted kinetic spear, who lived sometime in the late 10 and early 11 century DR.

Giddeon was a renowned researcher and philosopher of the natural arcane magic currents who developed a theory that stated that the world had natural "currents" of magical energy, and one could tap into these "currents." According to Giddeon's teachings, once a wizard could understand the "currents," they could use the flow of energy and draw from it via certain spells or focus devices. It was believed that Giddeon the Believer perished in a massive laboratory explosion of the, with only several items surviving. ## Gila monster

Gila monsters were a species of venomous and slow-moving lizard that could be found in the Plain of Standing Stones. They were counted among the most dangerous lizards that could be encountered in that region of the Anauroch desert.

Description
These small lizards had large heads with powerful jaws and around thirty-five grooved teeth. Their bodies measured  in length, with diminutive legs and short, stout tails. The scales of a gila monster were brightly colored and rough in texture, almost bead-like, forming gaudy patterns that never overlapped. They were noted by some as resembling muckdwellers, though moving in a quadrupedal manner instead of bipedally.

Behavior
Gila monsters generally had shy dispositions and wouldn't attack humanoids unless they were provoked.

Abilities
These lizards had a sack of venom within their mouths that flowed down their teeth when biting down on prey. This venom would inflict extreme pain upon a victim, followed by sweating, vomiting, swelling, and an unbearable ringing sound within the ears. These symptoms would gradually worsen until the victim fell to their death.

Combat
When engaging other creatures in combat, gila monsters fought by clamping down with their powerful jaws and administering lethal doses of their venom.

Ecology
Gila monsters lived solitary, nocturnal lives. They took shelter beneath rocks and within small burrows that they took over from other creatures.

Diet
Gila monsters had a largely carnivorous diet, consisting of small mammals, birds, and eggs. They could go without eating for a period of months, subsisting solely upon stores of fat within their tails.
