## Gibbering orb

Gibbering orbs were powerful floating aberrations that were theorized to be a common ancestor of gibbering mouthers and beholders.

Description
These huge orbs of at least  in diameter were pulsating masses of grayish flesh and hundreds of mouths and eyes of various sizes and shapes that moved, shifted, and swirled throughout the creatures' body. The eyes blinked as they swirled around the orb, and mouths screamed, drooled, and mumbled strange alien devotions. These creatures constricted and spasmed chaotically as if something was trapped inside the blobs, constantly trying to break out and escape as they floated above ground.

Behavior
Despite their chaotic, maddening appearance and incessant chanting, gibbering orbs were supremely intelligent beings. Some believed that the gibbering orbs functioned akin to a hive intelligent of several beings within one swirling aberrant body. They spoke all languages, and, in fact, each mouth spoke its own language, producing a maddening cacophony of voices that was sure to shatter any listener's mind. Even if gibbering orbs appeared incoherent, mad, and insane, the creatures were perversely rational. They had their own secret goals and objectives.

All living creatures were gibbering orbs' targets. The creatures attacked relentlessly with their eye rays, trying to consume the prey with their many mouths and absorb any magical knowledge and treasure they might have.

Abilities
Gibbering orbs' entire bodies were covered in moving eyes, giving them all-around vision as well as darkvision. Their bodies were buoyant and could fly but could never fall to the ground, slowly descending as if under the effects of the feather fall spell.

The creature's cacophonous gibbering could drive anyone mad within, similarly to the effects of the insanity spell.

Gibbering orbs had two dozen eyes that could release spell rays at their enemies. All of these spells were on par with magics unleashed by the most powerful wizards and had the range of . In battle, gibbering orbs could only target a single creature with five rays. The gibbering orb rays included: cone of cold, disintegrate, dominate monster, energy drain, feeblemind, finger of death, flesh to stone, greater dispelling, harm, hold monster, Abi-Dalzim's horrid wilting, implosion, inflict critical wounds, lightning bolt, magic missile, Mordenkainen's disjunction, Otto's irresistible dance, polymorph any object, power word, blind, power word, kill, power word, stun, prismatic spray, slay living, and temporal stasis.

Combat
When in battle, gibbering orbs extended pseudopods that ended with maws at lightning speeds to land devastating biting attacks on their prey. A single orb could extend four of such pseudopods at a single target and twelve pseudopods total within the timeframe of six seconds. To make the creatures even more terrifying – their eyes, similarly to beholders, could dispense a variety of deadly spells.

Any creature grabbed by a gibbering orb's pseudopods faced the danger of being swallowed whole. Gibbering orbs would consume any creature smaller than them. Once inside, the prey began being dissolved by the acid. However, they had a chance of cutting their way out. Those who failed to escape from the gibbering orb's ever-changing bodies were dissolved and consumed. If they had any spells prepared or memorized, the gibbering orb gained those spells to be cast freely. They could release such consumed spells at the rate of two per six seconds from the eyes. These consumed spells, if left unused, disappeared 24 hours later.

Ecology
Gibbering orbs were exceedingly rare solitary creatures that traveled between planes of existence, hunting for living creatures to kill and consume in their insatiable hunger. Very few who encountered these creatures lived long enough to tell the tale. The orbs originated from the Far Realm.

History
In the late 15 century DR, a gibbering orb was said to inhabit The Phelhelra, a fortress in Calimshan on the foothills of the Marching Mountains. The creature could be found in the deepest chambers – avoided by miners and adventurers alike. The origin of the gibbering orb was unknown. However, sages suggested that the monster slithered through a gate or some translocation magic.

## Giant four-armed gargoyle

Giant four-armed gargoyles, also known more simply as gargoyle maulers, were a unique mutation or variant of gargoyles for whom it was said only the lich Acererak knew the secrets for how to create. They were typically utilized by him as guardians over his tombs.

Description
These gargoyles on average stood around  tall, though could potentially be as large as, and weighed roughly . Besides their height, the most distinguishing feature these gargoyles exhibited was two sets of arms.

Abilities
These creatures could naturally see in areas of total darkness and were at times more perceptive than the standard gargoyle.The standard gargoyle of 5 edition is stated to have a passive perception of 10, while the giant four-armed gargoyle has a passive perception of 15. Though in 4 edition, this creature and the standard gargoyle both have a perception of +12. They could enter an inanimate stone form at will, granting them greater resistance to harm, regeneration from damage, and a short range tremorsense. While in this form they were entirely motionless, almost indistinguishable from a statue.

Combat
As was typical of most gargoyles, these creatures fought by rending their opponents with their ferocious claws and fangs.

Ecology
Languages
Being creatures of elemental earth, these gargoyle variants knew the languages of both Terran and Primordial.

History
After murdering the nine trickster gods of Omu and enslaving the city's citizenry to build for him a dungeon that would come to be known as the Tomb of the Nine Gods, Acererak stationed four of these gargoyles at the entrance to the dungeon's forth level, the bottom of a grand staircase. There the four sat perched on a cylindrical stone pedestal, atop a tile made of precious metal. Each pedestal measured  tall and  wide, was situated at one of the four cardinal directions, and had a slot in it  above the ground that was large enough to press a standard coin through.

Acererak had designed it so that each of the four pedestals was meant to take a certain denomination of currency corresponding to the type of precious metal atop them, though the gargoyles would also accept equivalent tithes of multiple coins, such as 10 copper pieces in place of a single silver piece, or coins that were of a greater value overall. The northern pedestal was meant to take a copper piece, the eastern pedestal a silver piece, the southern pedestal a gold piece, and the western pedestal a platinum piece. If their tithe was not paid or if any of their pedestals were damaged, the gargoyles would spring to life and attack those passing through.

On that same floor, Acererak also placed a giant four-armed gargoyle within a mirror of life trapping to act as one of its guardians. And on the fifth floor of the dungeon complex, Acererak had a statue of one of these creatures as part of a puzzle to obtain the Eye of Zaltec. This statue stood a measly  and was impossible to destroy. Those who plucked the Eye of Zaltec from its hands would open a nearby secret entrance, but also be met with the statue opening its mouth and filling the hallway with sleeping gas.

Sometime between 1488 DR and 1492 DR, a group of adventurers traveling through the Tomb of the Nine Gods encountered both sets of four-armed gargoyles. When they finally reached the statue on the fifth floor, they found that one of its four arms had broken off and was lying on the ground close to it.

## Giant black squirrel

Giant black squirrels were a large and predatory type of squirrel that lived in the dark parts of Cormanthor called the Starwood.

Description
These giant black squirrels were about the size of a small dog, far larger than the ordinary squirrels that were so common in many forests.

Behavior
They were malicious little critters that would attack helpless or very weak creatures, and were opportunistic thieves, stealing small shiny objects, like coins or jewelry, from those unaware creatures too strong to attack directly.

Ecology
Diet
Giant black squirrels were typically known to eat fruits, nuts, and grains, but would happily feast on the flesh of those weak enough to succumb to a scurry of these beasts.

Habitats
In northern Faerûn, they could be found in the deeper and darker parts of Cormanthor, always lairing 20 or more feet above the ground in the hollows of trees.

## Giant constrictor snake

A giant constrictor snake was a much larger version of the normal constrictor snake.

Description
The giant constrictor snake was around 45 feet (14 meters) long, with green scales that helped it blend in with its surroundings.

Abilities
So strong was this great snake that it would take at least four strong humans to even have a chance of uncoiling it from around a victim.

Subspecies
The White-furred snake, also called Snow Serpents, was a subtype of the giant constrictor snake.

Behavior
These snakes would usually hunt by dropping from above to grab their prey within their coils, then begin constricting and biting the prey. Once it had crushed and eaten its prey, it would sleep and digest for several days. They were known for being far more aggressive than their smaller cousins, owing to the greater amount of food they had to consume to survive.

Ecology
Habitat
The giant constrictor snake preferred wooded areas or swamps, where the trees and foliage were to its advantage when hunting, but it was adept at swimming and they had been encountered in warm tropical waters miles from land.

The Cormanthor forest in north Faerûn was a known haven for these creatures, as was the Vast Swamp near Cormyr.

In Kara-Tur they lived in the tropical and sub-tropical swamps and mountains.

Usage
The skin of a giant constrictor snake was too stiff and bulky to be workable for anything but armor.

Appendix

 Giant snake

## Giant dragonfly

Giant dragonflies were fearless and voracious hunters known to prey on any warm-blooded creature, as well as all other sorts of insects.

Description
The giant dragonfly was an insect of far larger proportions than their cousins the regular dragonfly, being 10 feet (3 meters) long with a wingspan of 10 feet (3 meter), though the body itself was just 2 feet (60 centimeters) wide. Some especially large giant dragonflies had 20 feet (6 meters) wingspans and body lengths. They had a remarkable brilliant, glittering coloration.

Behavior
They were fearless against anyone of smaller size than themselves and suffered from a voracious appetite. They were rather hard to hit with missile weaponry due to their quick and darting movement.

The skin of the giant dragonflies was used for apparel and decoration, and could be quite valuable if properly preserved.

Larva
The larva of the giant dragonfly, called nymphs, appeared to be toothless and inoffensive creatures that hid in the water during their metamorphosis into adults. However, when provoked by creatures nearby, they would attack from their hiding places with their hard and very functional mandibles.

Behavior
Always on the lookout for new prey, giant dragonflies were known to hunt humans and their like, thus posing a threat to the unwary. They would dart in and bite with their mandibles, then fly away and around so as to always face their opponents.

If their opponents were small or tiny, they would try to scoop them into their leg baskets and then simply devour them while on the wing.

Giant dragonflies living in the Dead Marsh of T'u Lung, usually hunted the native giant carp for food.

Ecology
Giant dragonflies were found from temperate to warm climes and lived in marshy areas and other watery areas.

Relationships
Female giant dragonflies could with time and effort be trained to allow people to ride them as flying mounts, if trained from hatching, while the males were far too territorial. Those who preferred to ride flying vermin, especially if they were raiders or bandits, preferred the giant dragonfly as they were rather docile compared to other fliers of such sort and had a very distinct thrumming sound that would announce the raiders' presence to the common people, striking fear and terror.

In 1358 DR, a jiki-ketsu-gaki who laired in the Dead Marsh, had a group of five giant dragonflies under his command that he used to hunt down humans.

The Witchlight Carnival used giant dragonflies to give flights to attendees.

Appendix
See Also
 Redwing: a massive dragonfly-like creature used for transportation in High Imaskar.
 Sapphire dragonfly: a blue dragonfly that could be found in northwest Faerûn.

## Gill

Gill was a member of the Order of the Burning Dawn in the late 15 century.

History
Around 1490 DR, Gill, along with fellow members of the Burning Dawn Neddy, Calick, and Bryn Lightfingers, was escorting a merchant caravan along the High Road between Neverwinter and Luskan, when the caravan was ambushed by the Snarling Fang mercenary company. The members of the Burning Dawn were brought to the hideout of the mercenaries and killed. Accused of being demons of the Abyss, Neddy and Gill were both executed by Sir Banagar, who carried out the sentence with a sword on the order of Javen Tarmikos.

Gin was a member of the Dead Rats gang in Luskan in the late 15 century.

History
Around 1490 DR, Gin was found dead by the Champion of the Burning Dawn in the Luskan sewers.

## Gilded imp

Gilded imps were a variety of imps from the Nine Hells specially blessed by the archdevil Mammon.

Description
Gilded imps resembled the regular variety, but had a second skin of gold.

Behavior
Mammon's golden blessing enhanced the charisma of gilded imps.

Abilities
The golden plating of gilded imps made them much tougher and difficult to hurt than normal imps. Their stingers could also inflict a condition known as "Mammon's Hold," which temporarily turned their victims into golden statues.

## Giregi Igiri

Giregi Igiri was a merchant who specialized in alchemical supplies and potions in Port Nyanzaru, Chult, during the late 15 century DR.

Personality
He was a difficult man to impress.

Activities
Giregi was a very busy merchant who worked for the merchant princes of Port Nyanzaru to facilitate the import and trade of alchemical reagents as well as finished potions, apothecary supplies, poisons, antidotes, and even certain foods and drinks.Given the scope of goods in this list, he likely represented the interests of the merchant princes Jessamine (for ingredients and poisons), Kwayothé (for food and drink), and Wakanga O'tamu (for potions). He worked alongside the Master of the Market as a point of contact for foreign merchants who sold such goods and were seeking to establish a presence in Port Nyanzaru, and he could be found at the city's Bazaar.

History
During the events of the death curse, Giregi helped to organize a seasonal symposium of Port Nyanzaru merchants and artisans in order to facilitate trade into the city.

## Girdles, Bloomers, and Hose

Girdles, Bloomers, and Hose was a shop in the Central Trade Lanes of Skullport in the late 14 century DR.

Structure
Girdles, Bloomers, and Hose was a one-story building in fair condition.

Services
Girdles, Bloomers, and Hose was known for its high-quality undergarments and intimate apparel. Though it once enjoyed a reputation as Skullport’s premier destination for personalized service, by 1370 DR the shop has seen a decline in its prominence. Despite this, it continueds to offer some of the finest quality goods in the city, albeit at premium prices.

Inhabitants
Girdles, Bloomers, and Hose was owned by Marni Sobor, a human and apprentice wizard who inherited the business following her mother’s tragic death in a street robbery. Cheerful and determined, Marni operated the shop primarily to support her two younger siblings.

## Gilda

Gilda was an innkeeper and a serving maid from The Golden Apple inn in the Docks District of Neverwinter in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Gilda was a pleasant enough older human woman with a light skin tone and light brown hair gathered in a loose bun.

History
Gilda was in the Golden Apple's employ by the . She could always be found in the inn's taproom, greeting guests and escorting them to their seats or rooms on the second floor. When priestess Tasina Alcrest was staying at the inn before her departure aboard the Midnight Rose, a group of thugs who were claiming to be Captain Allendry's men were escorted to the priestess' chambers by Gilda. The criminals were mercenaries hired by Tasina to guard her on the trip to Neverwinter and beyond. However, she had no coin, and the men were ready for violence. The true crewmen of Allendry's ship, Eater-of-Garm and Nisar Zayid, arrived just in time to get a key from Gilda, to receive an annoyed comment from her about too many of Allendry's men running around, and to save the priestess and escort her to the Rose.

## Giant tigerfish

Giant tigerfish were a species of predatory fish that inhabited inland waterways of Chult in southwest Faerûn.

Description
The name "tigerfish" was not a reference to the fish's appearance, but rather its behavior: it was said to have the ferocity of a tiger.

Ecology
Giant tigerfish were one of the deadliest predators in the River Soshenstar. They hunted kapenta fish, which were common near the mouth of Chult's rivers near Port Nyanzaru. As such, giant tigerfish were known to attack Chultan fishers as they angled for kapenta.

History
During the Amnian occupation of Port Nyanzaru in the 15 century DR, foreign sport fishers were known to travel to Chult seeking to land a giant tigerfish.

## Gigantopithecus

Gigantopithecus  was a rare species of ancient ape believed to be extinct.

Description
They were huge compared to most apes, standing at a height of  with very broad builds. They appeared as ogre-sized gorillas, with females weighing about  and having strength equivalent to that of a troll while males weighed somewhat more and had strength comparable to that of a hill giant.

Behavior
Gigantopitheci were similar to gorillas in temperament and habits. They were largely shy and peaceful creatures, making them harmless if given space. Their suspicious natures led them to avoid confrontation, but also meant that they were aggressive when threatened. If angered, they feared next to nothing aside from fire or large numbers of humans. They fought with their fists and teeth.

Ecology
While native to tropical forests like most apes, they were also said to have inhabited temperate regions and to have even dwelt in open environments. By the late 15 century DR, the only known living specimens dwelt in the jungles of Chult.

A band of gigantopitheci consisted of one male and several females with their offspring. Such bands could include up to twenty individuals, but more commonly consisted of between two and eight family members, with approximately one in five being juveniles.

Diet

They largely ate seeds, tubers, and bits of vegetation, and particularly enjoyed arrowroot. They might also eat small animals if they managed to catch them, and if they had access to a human settlement, they might raid farms.

Uses
The teeth of a gigantopithecus were regarded as trophies by Chultan hunters.

History
As of the mid–14 century DR, these apes were believed to be extinct, with only scattered bones attesting to their existence. Fossilized gigantopithecus teeth could be found in apothecary shops of Port Nyanzaru as of the late 15 century DR.

During the time of the death curse, a member of the Hunters' Guild of Chult named Desha of the Shadows learned of a gigantopithecus living in the ruins of Omu, and devised a plan to lure it out with a particularly massive sample of arrowroot.

## Gisentraxia

Gisentraxia was a bronze dragon in the late 15 century DR.

Description
In her human form, Gisentraxia appeared to be a light-skinned woman with brown eyes and hair. She wore face paint and had her hair gathered in a bun over an undershave.

Relationships
She was Palhavorithyn's mate and a friend of Elminster Aumar.

History
In the late 15 century DR, Gisentraxia aided her friend Elminster in investigating the Weave. Later, she carried Elminster to the Dragonbone Vale, where the Cult of the Dragon, allied with the Red Wizards of Thay, planned on making the prophecy of the Chronicle of Years to Come come true. She assumed her human form while assisting Sergeant Alphonse Knox's troops and adventurers from Neverwinter, who operated out of the Shield of the North camp. However, Gisentraxia had a personal stake in the cult's sinister plot; she believed that Palhavorithyn, her beloved, was corrupted by foul magics.

## Giant marine spider

The giant marine spider was a rare species of monstrous spider adapted to living in aquatic environments.

Description
These spiders came in a variety of sizes, but tended to be smaller than ordinary monstrous spiders, with the common size being around .

Behavior
Hunting parties of these spiders consisted of up to half a dozen members, working together to swarm and bring down their prey. When hunting, they would hide in vegetation like seaweed until a creature came close, when they would pounce, rushing forward and attempting to paralyze the victim with their highly venomous bite.

Ecology
Giant marine spiders built their huge nests from a mixture of webbing and local vegetation underneath the surface of the water. These were filled with air allowing the spiders to breathe while hiding away from most predators. Captured prey would be taken into these lairs to be cocooned and devoured at leisure.

Habitats
These creatures were known to only inhabit saltwater locales, especially the shallow seas in tropical regions where vegetation was plentiful. These creatures were known to be found in the warmer parts of the ocean off Kara-Tur.

Summoning
A giant marine spider could be summoned with the Summon monster V spell if the caster were in a saltwater aquatic environment. Sea focused spellcasters could summon up to 2 giant marine spiders with the same spell.

Appendix
See Also
Giant water spider the far more common species that lived in freshwater aquatic environments.

## Giant whip spider

The giant whip spider or amblypygus was a rare variety of arachnids that were roaming hunters rather than web users.

Description
The giant whip spider was a human-sized type of pedipalpi. They had flat segmented bodies that were covered in fur of a reddish brown color. They had six normal legs, long spiny pincers, and even longer whip-like feelers, from which they gained their name. Their entire bodies were shaped in such a way as to maintain a grip on their prey with these pincers, regardless of the force.

Behavior
They were far-wandering hunters, roaming fearlessly in search of food in groups of up to five, though would never hunt other arachnids of any kind. Against larger prey or opponents they would team up, but would otherwise hunt alone near their packs. They would use their whip-like appendages to probe fissures and other unseen areas for traps or prey.

They would mate once around every eight months, with the female producing a cluster of 2–40 soft-shelled eggs. These would be injected into the body of a disabled but living creature, often pinned under rocks and with its joints broken by the pedipalp couple. When the young hatched, they would then eat their way to freedom through the host creature and form their own roaming hunting groups. Though not having any venom of their own they were still immune to all sorts of poisons and corrosives.

Combat
Giant whip spiders fought with their two pincers alongside their vicious bite, attempting to grip their opponents in a crushing hold. Holding foes with their pincers, they would continue biting until anything they held was dead or they were. So tightly did they hold onto their prey that it was said that it was as hard to bend bars as it was to escape the grip of a giant whip spider.

Usage
The spined foreleg-pincers were often used by goblinkin to make maces, while the feelers were used to make whips.

The meat was not palatable for most creatures, but they could do so in desperate situations.

They were often used by drow as household guardians, pets, and as the equivalent of hunting dogs.

References

## Giant harvester termite

The giant harvester termite was a large insectoid creature.

Description
Giant harvester termites varied greatly in size, from the smallest workers of around  in length to the large kings and queens that were hidden away deep beneath the earth. The termite workers were small, feeble creatures focused on the endless maintenance of the colony. The soldiers were guards of both the hive and royalty, with the king being a particularly large soldier specimen. The queen was a bloated being that could barely move, spending her life producing new eggs for the hive.

Behavior
The termites were nocturnal and shunned light, moving around in great numbers of thirty or more workers to forage for plant material, accompanied by a few soldier termites. They would use the plant material as fodder for fungus that they cultivated on the walls of their underground galleries, and fed their young and rulers alike with the fungi.

Combat
In combat, only the soldier termites would actively fight against an enemy. While the workers would retreat into the hive, the soldier termites did not back down from invaders, no matter the numbers or chance of victory. While not intelligent, soldier termites had an innate sense of tactics due to their breeding. They would attempt to flank their foes whenever possible, aiding each other to bring down foes that were particularly difficult to strike. The soldiers had strong sword-like jaws that could cut an enemy in half with one bite, and were able to spit 1 pint (570 milliliters) of an irritating kerosene-like fluid against hostiles up to  away. Those hit by the fluid would be blinded for up to 2 minutes. If exposed to flame or other heat source, the fluid would burn, causing great damage to whoever was covered in it.

If intruders made it to either the egg or the royal chambers, then all termites, except special attendants and guards, would fight and fight even harder than they had elsewhere against any foe, with every able-bodied termite coming to reinforce their kin as an telepathic call would be sent out. The giant king termite had a defensive spit like the soldier termites but could spit twice the amount and twice the distance. The giant queen termite meanwhile had even less combat capability than the workers; she was barely able to shift herself around, relying exclusively on her guards for protection.

Ecology
Giant termites built nests consisting of stone-hard mounds around  in diameter and anywhere from  to  high, with numerous circular openings near the top. These openings led to diagonal, horizontal, and vertical passages, which in turn led to underground chambers that was the hive proper.

Inside the underground lair, there would be as many as ten times the workers and soldiers as encountered on the surface spread throughout the nest. The king and queen termites would be found only in the royal chambers, always located near the bottom of the hive, with the egg chamber nearby containing as many eggs as there were workers and soldiers combined. Near the royal chambers was placed a shaft leading to the repository well where unusable materials would be dumped—such as gold, bronze, and other worthless minerals.

Giant termites were found in various locations, from the tropical mountains and jungles of Kara-Tur to the desert wastes, and in the great Cormanthor forest. They mostly lived where it was warm and where large amounts of vegetation were available.

The worst enemy of the giant termites was the formians, as they hated the termites and enjoyed eating their eggs. If a giant termite hive detected formian raiders, they would send out the crisis telepathic call long before the raiders had reached the hive proper.

Abandoned termite mounds often served as lairs for various creatures in the wastes, as they made for excellent temperature-controlled lairs, at least until the lack of maintenance made the hive uninhabitable.

History
Around, a group of owlbears in the starwood of Cormanthor were facing extinction due to lack of food. That was until they stumbled upon a pit containing a fallen oak tree, infested with giant harvester termites. After crushing and eating those they could find and fish out by sticks, they left the rest of the nascent colony alone. A month later they returned and found the pit crawling with larvae, as thousands of termites had hatched since last the owlbears had been around. This time, the owlbear pack dumped rotting tree-limbs and wet leaves into the pit, then sat around and watched the small insects feed on the soft foliage. Every so often, an owlbear would eat a handful of larvae but otherwise would leave them alone. After a time, the owlbear pack decided it was much easier to eat the termites than to hunt wolves or serpents. Over the next few weeks, the owlbears kept dumping rotten wood into the pit and the termite colony kept growing. Any soldier termite would be killed and eaten as soon as it hatched, due to the annoying habit of spewing their flammable liquid on the owlbears. More pits were then dug by the owlbears, and more giant harvester termite colonies were established by leaving adult termites in these new pits. Other owlbear packs soon copied the practice and throughout the starwood many owlbears subsisted on homegrown termites. The owlbear population not only stabilized, but slowly began to expand.

Moreover, the owlbears ate the adult termites by crushing them, so as to get to the tender innards, then tossed the empty shells back into the pits. A fortuitous effect of mixing owlbear saliva with decomposing termite shells was an aroma that was near-irresistible to wild horses. The owlbears soon learned to hide behind trees near their termite pits, then pounced on the horses who came to investigate the smell, and tossed the remains of the carcass into the termite pit after eating their fill.

But pyrolisks were also attracted to the termite pits and, while the owlbears despised their trespassing, they would rather give up a termite pit than risk incineration. Once a pyrolisk hen located an acceptable termite pit, she would eat all the adult termites, then decorate her new nest by scattering a few shiny objects like gems. After laying an egg or two, the pyrolisk hen then abandoned the nest. The hatchlings would spend the first few weeks of their lives eating termite larvae until they were old enough to fend for themselves and leave the termite pit.

If one of the termite pits happened to be set on fire, it would spew a geyser of flame some  into the air, killing all termites and any pyrolisk hatchlings caught within.

## Gilbert

Gilbert was a friendly dwarvish barbarian and student pf the Neverwinter Academy in Neverwinter during the late 14 century DR.

Relationships
Gilbert attended the same martial combat classes as the person who would become the Hero of Neverwinter. Some rumors said that he was attracted to the future Hero and proposed to attend the feast that followed the graduation ceremony together.

He admired Aribeth de Tylmarande and considered her to be a hero and an inspiration. He also harbored a crush on the paladin and was jealous of fellow student who graduated and got an opportunity to meet her.

History
Gilbert was one of the Neverwinter Academy students in the  during the deadly outbreak of the Wailing Death. Although Gilbert was capable fighter, his life was tragically cut short in the attack on the Academy.

## Giant venomous snake

The giant venomous snake was a larger and often more deadly version of the normal venomous snake, being as large or larger than most humans and demihumans.

Behavior
Like their lesser cousins, these great venomous snakes would attack prey by injecting it with venom through their hollow fangs, but some species could also constrict lesser creatures like halflings. They would roam in groups of up to 6 individuals, biting their targets with deadly poison and afterwards swallowing their prey whole.

Species
 Black mamba A very aggressive snake that could grow to over  in length.
 Bushmaster A powerful form of viper.
 Gaboon A large-fanged viper that blended with its jungle environment.
 Giant cobra A larger more intelligent species of cobra.
 Jit snake A large dangerous and venomous snake that dwelt in Cormanthor.
 King cobra A very large cobra that could reach up to  in length.
 Viper A common type of venomous snake that came in various sizes and colorings.

Ecology
Habitat
Giant venomous snakes inhabited the Starwood parts of the Cormanthor forest. They were also encountered deep in the Raurin desert in the Temple of Set.

## Gilles

Gilles was a malicious Talonite who sought to undermine the Tyrran faith in the City Core of Neverwinter in the late 14 century DR.

Description
Errol was a bearded human man with graying hair and cold unfriendly manner.

Activities
Gilles had eyes and ears around the city, spying for the Church of Talona.

Activities

During the time the Wailing Death plagued Neverwinter in the, Gilles frequented the Moonstone Mask in the City Core. He was searching for someone with no conscience to work for him against the Church of Tyr. While Oleff Uskar of the Hall of Justice tried to recruit adventurers to find the tomb and ancient artifacts of the founder of Neverwinter, Halueth Never, to strengthen Neverwintran morale and maybe, just maybe, cure the plague, Gilles hoped to convince them to bring the artifacts to him for a hefty reward. Gilles planned on using them to benefit the Church of Talona and possibly, wrap the artifacts to the evil deity's will. Gilles also visited the dig site in the Peninsula District a stone a fanciful quill from the priests who ran the excavations. The priest's name was Jacob and he was already dead by the time Gilles got to roll the body over.

## Giant caterpillar

Giant caterpillars were a species of somewhat common giant insects that inhabited the Sword Coast North.

Description
Similarly to mundane caterpillars, the giant varieties had colorful hides, from bright greens and browns to striking blacks. Some powerful varieties were known to have large venomous spikes on their bodies and deadly venomous pincers.

Ecology
Giant caterpillars inhabited the Underdark and caves near Mantol-Derith, subterranean areas near farmlands and the town of Triboar, the sewers of Waterdeep, mines deep underneath Termalaine, under the Sunblight citadel, the Wyrmheart Mine in Chult, and in the depths of Feywild.

Giant caterpillars dwelt in caverns, feeding on fungus. They responded with aggression if their foraging grounds were disturbed. The larger species of giant spiked caterpillars could trigger a feeding frenzy among the smaller kin through pheromone discharge.

Giant caterpillars produced silk, which was weaved into exceptionally strong ropes, making them targets for industrious adventurers of Waterdeep. Their poison sacs were also harvested to be used as magic ritual ingredients.

## Giant snail

Giant snails were extremely large versions of the typical garden variety snail.

Description
Much like the common snail, these creatures were quite slow and plodding in their movements.

Abilities
These creatures could see in areas of low illumination to total darkness. Much like the common snail, they could be harmed by salt, though owing to their size it took contact with  or more of salt to do serious harm.

Combat
These mollusks typically retreated into their hard shells when threatened. When forced to fight back, a giant snail would simply slam its body against its aggressor.

Notable Giant Snails
 Spirelli, an intelligent giant snail capable of conversing in Common who was a member of the Blade Council of Lomatra in the early 13 century DR.

Ecology
Habitats
These creatures were known to be found naturally in the Feywild, the Great Bhaerynden region of the Underdark, and the swamp of Shibinuma on the island of Shinkoku.

Usages
Some creatures in the Feywild, like Agdon Longscarf and his band of harengon brigands, forced giant snails into service as pack animals.

The Witchlight Carnival kept a number of giant snails for the purposes of racing. Having their own dedicated,  circular racecourse, the racing of giant snails proved to be a highly competitive and crowd-pleasing attraction for the carnival. These snails were notably faster than the average giant snail. Each competitor was outfitted with colored caparisons and a numbered saddle strapped to their shell.

On an unknown world, the shells of enormous snails were harvested for the creation of nautiloids.

History
On one famed occasion the Company of the Cleaver delved into the depths of Undermountain, the grand dungeon underneath Waterdeep, and through some series of events their adventure concluded with the members of the group riding a giant silvery snail along the streets of the city's Castle Ward.

Trivia
The caves of Daerndar, in the Grand Duchy of Shantal of the Border Kingdoms, often produced outbursts of wild magic effects whenever magical discharges occurred within them. One such effect that could occur was a baleful polymorph, which had a high likelihood of turning a nearby being into a giant snail.

## Giant nautilus

Giant nautiluses were an extra-large variety of their diminutive cousins native to the Elemental Plane of Water.

Description
Giant nautiluses looked much like the ordinary nautiluses that were found in the oceans. Adult giant nautiluses had spirally coiled shells of  in diameter, and twenty strong tentacles encircled the mouths of the giant nautiluses that they used for every kind of manipulation necessary.

Personality
Often called "druids of the deep" for their absolute neutrality and great insistence on maintaining the balance of the underwater world, they were known to be used by tritons, merfolk, and locathah as impartial judges to mediate disputes among themselves and the different races. They considered sunken ships to be terrible eyesores and would actively help in removing them, even going so far as to protect those who were removing the ships from the great predators that lived in the depths. While they were totally uninterested in treasures of any kind, that were not stupid nor naive, and understood the concepts of avarice and greed. Thus they would demand that sunken vessels carrying mundane cargo be removed before they allowed those storing treasure to be taken away, and that the entire vessel be removed, not just the cargo.

Giant nautiluses seldom attacked humans or any other intelligent ocean-faring or ocean-dwelling race unless in self-defense—even the sahuagin and ixitxachitl were safe as long as they refrained from attacking. For all of their peaceful ways, however, giant nautiluses were quick to stop any who overfished or polluted the areas where they held sway. If their pleas and attempted negotiations went nowhere, they would intimidate the offenders. If even that went on deaf ears, they would resort to force, dragging down small ships and rocking the larger vessels until damaged.

Combat
In combat, giant nautiluses would attempt to constrict their foes with some of their tentacles, and bite their victims if they could drag them into their mouths. They additionally used their spells to either pacify or slay their foes.

Abilities
They were very long-lived, with a natural lifespan of between 3,000 and 4,000 years. They had a form of telepathy that allowed them to communicate with any intelligent creature, regardless of languages spoken and understood.

Giant nautiluses had a constant know alignment spell-effect and could cast charm person or mammal, conjure fish, control temperature, 10' radius, detect evil and good, detect magic, fish summoning I or summon monster I, fish summoning II or summon monster II, fish summoning III or summon monster III, locate fish or plants, lower water, and wall of coral at will. They were also able to cast conjure water elemental thrice a day.

Society

Natives to the Plane of Water, they wandered the depths of the deepest oceans on the Prime Material plane. They were also encountered in Thalasia, the great sea that occupied a layer of Elysium; in Lunia, on the first layer of the Mount Celestia; and in Aquallor, in the depths of Poseidon's realm Caletto.

Solitary wanderers of the deepest depths, giant nautiluses maintained neither lairs nor permanent abodes, using magic to return to the Plane of Water when they wished to mate and reproduce. As carnivores, they preyed on huge crustaceans such as giant crabs and giant lobsters. They often saw krakens as foes, owing to the latter's tendency to have grandiose schemes of underwater empire, which ultimately always came at the detriment of the environment.

Usage
The shells of giant nautiluses were great prizes, able to be used as roomy, near-indestructible submarines for exploring the depths of the oceans.

## Girmond Dyle

Girmond Dyle was an elven Harper agent active in Cormyr in the late 15 century DR.

Description
He had black hair and pale skin, and wore brown armor.

History

Following the Shadovar invasion of Wheloon circa 1485 DR and Oriphaun Huntsilver's disappearance with a Nether Scroll, Girmond was sent to meet with the Cormyrean-aligned adventurers who had gathered up the Scroll pieces after finding them across the High Road. All of the remaining fragments were in the hands of the Netherese forces occupying Granwell Keep within the Blooded Wood, and so he sent them to assault the Keep and claim them while he safeguarded those the Harpers had taken. However, he was shortly after discovered and killed by the Netherese forces, who claimed the pieces of the Nether Scroll for themselves and put his corpse on display for the Cormyrean forces to find at the entrance of the Keep.

## Giant eel

Giant eels were a monstrous version of the commonly encountered eel, often resembling morays.

Description
Like most other types of eels they were long snake-like fish, often around  long when adult.

Behavior
Giant eels were more ill-tempered than other types of eels.

Combat
Lacking the electric ability of the electric eels and great marine eels, giant eels would instead bite with their pointy teeth, striking with incredible speed.

Ecology
Habitat
While they predominantly lived in saltwater, around 1-in-10 giant eels were at home in freshwater instead. Fresh water giant eels were found in the Stump Bog near the Dessarin river.

Usage
The skin of giant eels was used to bind certain books, the binding being smooth, glossy, and turning black with time.

Locathah would ride atop giant eels wielding light lances in combat.

Some also used giant eels for towing ships through water in the Sea of Fallen Stars, where the giant eels could tow up to  each, thus often requiring multiple giant eels working together to tow the ships.

Summoning
A giant eel could be summoned in freshwater environments with the summon monster V Spell.

History
Giant eels fought as part of Iakhovas's forces assaulting Voalidru in the  during the Twelfth Serôs War.

## Gizla

Gizla was a scholastic woman in Eveningstar during the 1480s DR.

Description
Gizla was a big half-orc woman, with long black hair and tan skin.

History

During the Sschindylryn invasion of Eveningstar, Gizla heard of the strange and terrible events around Lolth and the Rift Between Worlds, which Lolth was using to slowly devour the world of Eberron. She investigated the issue until it culminated with Valana's efforts in the Demonweb Pits.
